The CoreDNS-derived test asserts it, but neither `crates/pharos-plugins/src/file/README.md` nor `docs/compatibility.md` records it, and ADR 0002 requires such differences to be recorded. | No |
| §3 Negative Answers from Authoritative Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:445-457,827-853` | **Violation**: File-backed negative responses copy the SOA with its original TTL. They do not cap SOA TTL at `min(SOA TTL, SOA.MINIMUM)`, so a negative answer can outlive its specified negative TTL. | No |
| §3 Negative Answers from Authoritative Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:855-867` | **Partial**: Signed wildcard NODATA gets an NSEC for the wildcard owner, but no NSEC proving that no closer name exists. RFC 4035 §3.1.3.4 requires both proofs; the comment cites §3.1.3.1, which covers ordinary NODATA. | No |
| §3 Negative Answers from Authoritative Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:459-467` | **Violation**: File-backed signed negative responses copy the SOA's RRSIG TTL unchanged. RFC 2308 says the signature TTL should be trimmed in line with the SOA TTL. | No |
| §3 Negative Answers from Authoritative Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:803-809,1085-1114,1284-1299` | **Partial**: An ExternalName target NXDOMAIN is returned with the service's NOERROR code and without the target's SOA authority record, so the final negative answer is not represented for caching. | No |
| §3 Negative Answers from Authoritative Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/mod.rs:692-716`, `crates/pharos-plugins/tests/functional/template_coredns.rs:264-295` | **Violation**: An authoritative NXDOMAIN template writes the operator's SOA into Answer rather than Authority. ADR 0002 would allow a CoreDNS-compatible difference only if recorded, and neither `docs/compatibility.md` nor `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/README.md` records it. | No |
| §3.1 Retries and Timeouts | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/setup.rs:111-123`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:288-303` | **Violation**: `max_connect_attempts` accepts values above three, and zero permits retries until the five-second deadline. A single upstream can therefore receive more than three queries over one transport before Pharos marks it unresponsive. | No |
| §3.2 Caching | `—` | **Missing**: Cache documentation does not describe which resolution failures are cached, the cache-key choices, or expected failure-cache behavior for operators. | No |
| §3.2 Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:201-209`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:521-533` | **Partial**: The default failure TTL is 5 seconds, but `servfail 0`, sub-second TTLs, or `disable denial` can prevent caching or reduce the TTL below 1 second. RFC 9520 requires failure caching for at least 1 second. | No |
| §3.2 Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:521-559` | **Violation**: RFC 9520 recommends exponential or linear backoff for persistent failures; Pharos retries with no failure-duration backoff. | No |
| §3.2 Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:521-559`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:201-209` | **Partial**: Cache handles SERVFAIL and plugin errors, but not all resolution failures, such as REFUSED or FORMERR. `servfail 0` and sub-second values bypass the one-second minimum; the five-minute maximum is enforced. | No |
| §4 SOA Minimum Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone.rs:443-474` | **Violation**: `$TTL` supplies omitted TTLs for legacy SIG records, but RFC 2308 requires an omitted SIG TTL to come from the SIG record's Original TTL. | No |
| §5 Caching Negative Answers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:157-160` | **Violation**: NXDOMAIN entries include QTYPE in the key and exact-match check. RFC 2308 §5 and RFC 4035 §4.5 use `<QNAME,QCLASS>` for name errors, so another QTYPE cannot reuse the cached answer. | No |
| §5 Caching Negative Answers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:605-619` | **Violation**: The cache first limits negative TTL by SOA TTL and SOA.MINIMUM, then raises it to the configured minimum (5 seconds by default). A 1-second negative TTL can therefore be served for 5 seconds. | No |
| §5 Caching Negative Answers | `crates/pharos-proto/src/response.rs:70-96`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:573-650` | **Violation**: NOERROR with no answers and no SOA or NS is classified as Positive, then cached. RFC 2308 §5 says negative responses without SOA should not be cached. | No |
| §5 Caching Negative Answers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:81-109` | **Partial**: The default negative-cache maximum is below the positive maximum and is configurable, but configuration permits setting it higher than the positive maximum. RFC 2308 says the negative limit should not exceed the positive limit. | No |
| §6 Negative answers from the cache | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:179-187` | **Partial**: Normal hits decrement cached negative TTL by age, but `keepttl` returns the original TTL. That lets cached SOA records retain a positive TTL after their cache age should have reduced it. | No |
| §7.1 Server Failure | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Violation**: Failure entries use QNAME, QTYPE, and QCLASS, but omit the required upstream server IP from the RFC 2308 cache tuple. | No |
| §7.2 Dead / Unreachable Server | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/health.rs:93-145`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:307-320` | **Violation**: A failed health probe marks an upstream IP down for all queries until a probe succeeds. This state has no five-minute expiry or query-tuple scope, and probe failures include more than explicit transport indications that the IP is unreachable. | No |
| §8 Changes from RFC 1034 | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:211-235`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:642-648` | **Partial**: Negative caching works by default, but `disable denial` can turn it off while positive caching remains enabled. RFC 2308 §8 requires negative caching when a resolver caches other answers. | No |
| §8 Changes from RFC 1034 | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:157-162` | **Violation**: NXDOMAIN keys include QTYPE and use the original Question name. RFC 2308 defines its negative-response QNAME as the final name after CNAME processing and keys NXDOMAIN by QNAME and QCLASS, so another QTYPE or a direct query for that final name misses. | No |

## RFC 2535: Domain Name System Security Extensions

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| RFC 2535 §5.2 NXT RDATA Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:212-223`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1003-1026` | **Violation**: NXT encoder writes the RFC 4034 §4.1.2 window-block bitmap (window, length, bitmap), not the RFC 2535 §5.2 unwindowed bitmap (bit n = type n, bit 30 always on, four-octet minimum, no trailing zero octets). For `next. A NS`, output is `\x00\x01\x60` (test `zone_legacy.rs:154`), which an RFC 2535 reader decodes as types 15, 17, 18. Fragment NA was wrong: RFC 3755 §3 keeps the RFC 2535 §5.2 syntax for type 30. No ADR or README records this; ADR 0002 needs deployment evidence and `crates/pharos/compatibility.md` omits it. Miscite: `legacy.rs:212` names RFC 4034 §4.1.2 (NSEC), so Documented is No. | No |

## RFC 2606: Reserved Top Level DNS Names

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2 TLDs for Testing, & Documentation Examples | — | **Violation**: RFC 6761 §6.2 updates this reservation: caching servers should not query upstream for `.test` by default and should return immediate negative responses. No reserved-name handling exists; `forward` routes by configured zone and can forward these queries. No recorded exception covers this behavior. | No |
| §2 TLDs for Testing, & Documentation Examples | — | **Violation**: RFC 6761 §6.2 says caching servers should offer a disabled-by-default option to enable upstream resolution for `.test`. No such special-name option exists. | No |
| §2 TLDs for Testing, & Documentation Examples | — | **Violation**: RFC 6761 §6.2 says authoritative servers should recognize `.test` names as special and return immediate negative responses by default. No reserved-name handling exists; the `file` plugin refuses or forwards names outside configured zones. No recorded exception covers this behavior. | No |
| §2 TLDs for Testing, & Documentation Examples | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:75-77,121-177` | **Partial**: The optional `local` plugin returns loopback addresses and negative responses for other localhost queries, but servers without this plugin can forward or refuse them. | Yes |
| §2 TLDs for Testing, & Documentation Examples | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:75-77,121-177` | **Partial**: The optional `local` plugin handles authoritative localhost queries, but zones served without this plugin can return configured data instead. | Yes |
| §2 TLDs for Testing, & Documentation Examples | — | **Violation**: RFC 6761 §6.4 says caching servers should not query upstream for `.invalid` names and should return immediate NXDOMAIN responses. No reserved-name handling exists. | No |
| §2 TLDs for Testing, & Documentation Examples | — | **Violation**: RFC 6761 §6.4 says authoritative servers should handle `.invalid` names as caching servers do. Configured zones can return positive answers, with no default special handling. | No |

## RFC 2782: A DNS RR for specifying the location of services (DNS SRV)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §The format of the SRV RR | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/whoami/mod.rs:41-59` | **Violation**: `whoami` emits `_udp.Name` or `_tcp.Name`, omitting the required `_Service` label from `_Service._Proto.Name`. Its README does not document this format exception. | No |
| §The format of the SRV RR | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/whoami/mod.rs:30, crates/pharos-plugins/src/whoami/mod.rs:87-94` | **Violation**: SRV records occur in class IN, but whoami copies the query class and can emit SRV in class CH. The plugin README does not document this class exception. | No |
| §The format of the SRV RR | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/transfer.rs:175-204, 238-280` | **Partial**: Hostname-valued external IPs produce SRV targets without target addresses in the transfer; an external target may resolve independently, but this code does not ensure an address RRset or prevent a CNAME target. Its RFC 2782 citation says nonexistent §3. | No |
| §The format of the SRV RR | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/mod.rs:317-347, 371-432` | **Partial**: Hostname-valued endpoints produce SRV targets even when Self Lookup yields no address or only a CNAME. RFC 2782 requires target address records and forbids alias targets; the code cites nonexistent §3. | No |

## RFC 2930: Secret Key Establishment for DNS (TKEY RR)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2 The TKEY Resource Record | `crates/pharos-proto/src/codes.rs:173, crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:30-32,963,1261-1296` | **Partial**: Pharos assigns TKEY type code 249 and its zone-file encoder emits the field sequence, but wire RDATA parsing rejects TKEY as an unsupported name-bearing type, and the generic RDATA writer rejects it. Pharos therefore cannot parse or build TKEY RRs in DNS messages. | No |
| §2.3 The Algorithm Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:200-218`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:963` | **Partial**: Zone input encodes an absolute algorithm name, but typed TKEY RDATA parsing rejects TKEY, so Pharos cannot consume the field for key derivation. The existing citation names §2, not §2.3. | No |
| §2.6 The Error Field — extended RCODE | `crates/pharos-proto/src/codes.rs:47-81`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:963` | **Partial**: The shared 16-bit `Rcode` includes TKEY codes 19–21, consistent with RFC 6895 §2.3's unified error space, but typed TKEY RDATA parsing rejects the Error field. | No |
| §2.7 The Key Size and Data Fields | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:209-212`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:963` | **Partial**: Zone input writes a network-order 16-bit size, but parses the size as `u8` and does not check that it matches the opaque key bytes. Typed TKEY RDATA parsing rejects the field. The existing citation names §2, not §2.7. | No |
| §2.8 The Other Size and Data Fields | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:213-218`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:963` | **Partial**: Zone input writes a network-order 16-bit size, but parses the size as `u8` and does not check that it matches the opaque Other Data. Typed TKEY RDATA parsing rejects the RR; the existing citation names §2, not §2.8. | No |
| §3 General TKEY Considerations — one TKEY per message | `crates/pharos-proto/src/message.rs:217-237` | **Violation**: Message parsing checks OPT placement and multiplicity, but imposes no TKEY count limit. It accepts messages with multiple TKEY RRs. | No |
| §3 General TKEY Considerations — transient, not cached or zone data | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:153-169, 561-608`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:301-335`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:200-217`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:145-188` | **Violation**: Cache intake excludes TSIG but not TKEY, and cache items retain other records. Zone parsing accepts TKEY, and zone construction retains it instead of rejecting this meta-RR. | No |
| §3 General TKEY Considerations — unavailable state | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:45-65`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/update.rs:5-17,97,152-153,170` | **Partial**: Admission treats TKEY as an ordinary QUERY and passes it to routing with no NOTIMP or REFUSED. Only the dynamic UPDATE path refuses TKEY, and it returns FORMERR rather than NOTIMP or REFUSED. | No |

## RFC 3007: Secure Domain Name System (DNS) Dynamic Update

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §1.4 Data and message signatures | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/store.rs:66-80`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:27-47` | **Partial**: dynamic updates commit changed Zone records without applying zone-key signatures. DNSSEC signs responses only when configured keys exist and the Query has DO set, so an update to a signed Zone is not guaranteed signed when the transaction completes. | No |
| §3.1 Standard policies | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/setup.rs:261-275` | **Partial**: Rules support one exact owner, the zone apex (`@`), or every zone name (`*`). They lack a concise rule for a principal's own name and subdomains. | No |
| §3.1 Standard policies | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/setup.rs:276-286` | **Partial**: `*` represents all types, but configuration has no concise user-types selector. | No |
| §4.3 Non-explicit updates to SIGs | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/store.rs:66-79`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:32-47` | **Partial**: Dynamic updates commit changed RRsets without signatures. The DNSSEC plugin signs selected responses only after a query with DO; it does not ensure the updated RRset is signed when the update completes. | No |
| §4.4 Effects on the zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/update.rs:184-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:116-151` | **Partial**: A changed zone gets a new SOA serial, but the update transaction does not regenerate or sign SOA and denial records. DNSSEC only synthesizes denial NSEC and signs selected records in later DO responses. | No |

## RFC 3110: RSA/SHA-1 SIGs and RSA KEYs in the Domain Name System (DNS)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2 RSA Public KEY Resource Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:319-326` | **Violation**: The parser accepts the three-octet exponent-length form even when the exponent is at most 255 bytes; RFC 3110 requires the one-octet form for those lengths. A signing key loaded this way retains and republishes that noncanonical DNSKEY data. | Yes |
| §2 RSA Public KEY Resource Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:333-350` | **Violation**: Leading zero octets in the exponent or modulus are silently stripped for key comparison, not rejected as RFC 3110 requires. The original public key remains in the loaded DNSKEY and can be published by the signer. | Yes |
| §2 RSA Public KEY Resource Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/sign.rs:77-85`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:129-149, 199-204` | **Violation**: RSA key loading accepts keys up to 8192 bits, but RFC 3110 §2 limits exponent and modulus to 4096 bits. RFC 5702 §§2.1–2.2 carries this limit to RSA/SHA-2 keys, which Pharos loads and signs. | No |

## RFC 3225: Indicating Resolver Support of DNSSEC

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3 Protocol Changes | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:459-467`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/builder.rs:328-334` | **Violation**: Updated RFC 4035 §3.1.1 says a server MUST NOT set TC solely because Additional-section RRSIGs do not fit. `MessageBuilder` sets TC on every size-limit failure, including an Additional RRSIG after its RRset fit. | Yes |
| §3 Protocol Changes | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/transfer.rs:59-65`, `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:233-235` | **Partial**: Normal Responses copy the query's DO bit during OPT scrubbing, but TCP AXFR/IXFR streams bypass scrubbing and build Messages without OPT. Transfer responses therefore omit the query's DO bit. | No |
| §3 Protocol Changes | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:855-867`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:931-948` | **Violation**: Updated RFC 4035 §3.1.3.4 requires NSEC proofs for both the absent wildcard type and absence of a closer match in wildcard NODATA. This path writes only the wildcard owner's NSEC; the nearby citation names §3.1.3.1, which covers exact-name NODATA. | No |
| §3 Protocol Changes | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:950-961` | **Violation**: Updated RFC 4035 §3.1.4 requires an NSEC RR and its RRSIG when a DO-set referral has no DS RRset. This code only attempts to write DS, so it emits no authenticated proof. | No |

## RFC 3226: DNSSEC and IPv6 A6 aware server/resolver message size requirements

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3 Protocol changes | `crates/pharos-server/src/udp.rs:139-142`, `crates/pharos-server/src/udp_linux.rs:57-71` | **Partial**: RFC 4035 §3 updates DNSSEC servers to fragment IPv6 UDP datagrams at the minimum IPv6 MTU when needed and the path MTU is unknown. These send paths use generic UDP socket calls and do not ensure that fragmentation behavior; they rely on platform defaults. | No |
| §3 Protocol changes | `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:275-281`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/edns.rs:152-153` | **Violation**: DNSSEC-capable servers MUST advertise a message size of at least 1220 octets and SHOULD advertise 4000. Pharos never advertises its own maximum: the response OPT copies the query's UDP payload size (`source.size = self.size`, outcome.rs:281), so a 512-octet requester gets 512 back. No ADR or plugin README records this. | No |

Pharos does not execute substitution expressions, so code-point matching and locale independence do not apply. | No |
| §4.1 NAPTR RR Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:773-791, crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:202-210` | **Violation**: Wire and master-file parsing accept arbitrary FLAGS octets; the RFC limits flags to ASCII letters and digits, case-insensitively, or an empty string. | No |

The RFC says this combination is erroneous and SHOULD be ignored or return an error. | No |

## RFC 3596: DNS Extensions to Support IP Version 6

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1 AAAA record type | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone.rs:898-909`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:277-287,454-457` | **Violation**: Zone parsing accepts AAAA records in non-IN classes, indexes records by type without class, and serves their stored class, including to IN queries. RFC 3596 §2.1 defines AAAA only for the Internet class. | No |
| §2.1 AAAA record type | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/whoami/mod.rs:25-30,71-82` | **Violation**: `whoami` copies the query class onto its AAAA record, so a CH query gets a CH AAAA, which §2.1 forbids. ADR 0002 allows CoreDNS-matching only where deployments depend on it, and no compatibility or plugin README entry records that. | No |
| §2.2 AAAA data format | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/mod.rs:892-899,931-938`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/transfer.rs:162-172` | **Violation**: Missing external NS addresses produce AAAA records with empty RDATA (NS Additional at 892-899, NS-subdomain answers at 931-938), not a 128-bit IPv6 address. The transfer helper repeats this at 171; its §2.2 citation at 163 covers byte order only, not the empty case. | No |
| §2.5 IP6.ARPA Domain | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/reverse.rs:35-49` | **Violation**: The parser accepts 33–35 address labels and ignores leading labels, but an IPv6 reverse name represents one address and so has exactly 32 nibbles before `ip6.arpa`. The comment at line 35 cites §2.5 next to this lenient rule, which §2.5 does not grant. | No |

## RFC 3597: Handling of Unknown DNS Resource Record (RR) Types

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2 Definition | `crates/pharos-proto/src/message.rs:87-89`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:643-965` | **Partial**: `Rdata::parse_in_message` classifies by type only, so it cannot distinguish class-specific RDATA formats. Its fallback also treats unhandled assigned QTYPE/meta-type values as `Unknown`, despite §2 excluding them. | No |
| §3 Transparency | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:409-501,723` | **Violation**: Known-type RDATA written in generic `\#` form is normalized instead of preserved exactly (malformed A, MX, NS, SOA and DS data is padded, truncated or accepted), so servers do not keep the RDATA of known types as received. Same code as the §5 row. | No |
| §4 Domain Name Compression | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:8-32,963` | **Partial**: the parser rejects several RFC 1035 well-known name-bearing types, including MD, MF, MB, MG, MR, and MINFO, rather than decompressing their embedded names as required. | No |
| §4 Domain Name Compression | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:8-32,963` | **Partial**: the parser also rejects RP, AFSDB, RT, SIG, PX, and NXT, so it does not provide the recommended decompression for these types; SRV and NAPTR are supported. | No |
| §5 Text Representation | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone.rs:898-909` | **Violation**: `parse_class` delegates directly to integer parsing, which accepts a leading `+`; thus invalid forms such as `CLASS+1` are accepted instead of requiring decimal digits immediately after `CLASS`. | Yes |
| §5 Text Representation | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:88-99` | **Partial**: generic RDATA words are concatenated before hex validation. This checks only the combined digit count and accepts separate words with odd digit counts, contrary to the requirement that each word contain an even number of hex digits. | Yes |
| §5 Text Representation | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:409-501,723`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_strictness.rs:80-129` | **Violation**: Generic known-type RDATA is normalized instead of preserving valid type-specific wire data; malformed A, MX, NS, SOA, and DS encodings are accepted, padded, or truncated. | Yes |
| §6 Equality Comparison | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/update.rs:87-132,181-205`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/setup.rs:63-147` | **Partial**: `same_record` compares opaque RDATA byte-for-byte, but the type whitelist rejects unknown types before UPDATE or prerequisite equality can run. | No |
| §7 DNSSEC Canonical Form and Ordering | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:8-32`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/canonical.rs:293-295` | **Partial**: canonicalization handles some listed legacy name-bearing types, but the parser rejects MD, MF, MB, MG, MR, MINFO, RP, AFSDB, RT, SIG, PX, NXT, and KX, while the canonicalizer rejects A6. Pharos therefore cannot produce the required canonical RDATA for all pre-RFC 3597 types retained by RFC 4034 §6.2. | No |

## RFC 4007: IPv6 Scoped Address Architecture

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §5 Specification (updates RFC 4007) | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/setup.rs:245-249,309-319` | **Deviation**: The `forward` Corefile directive accepts scoped non-global IPv6 upstreams but strips the zone before storing the socket address, so users cannot select the required zone. `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/README.md` documents this CoreDNS-compatible behavior. | Yes |
| §7 Sending Packets | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/setup.rs:245-249,261-272,280-295,309-319` | **Deviation**: `forward` strips zone identifiers from numeric upstreams and resolver entries, so scoped destinations cannot select the intended zone. The plugin README records this CoreDNS-compatible behavior, which ADR 0002 permits. | Yes |
| §11 Textual Representation | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/bind/mod.rs:42-58`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/setup.rs:245-319`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/acl/mod.rs:104-120,195-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/expression/mod.rs:777-819` | **Partial**: `bind` rejects a literal `<address>%<zone_id>`, while `forward` removes zones from numeric upstreams. Views emit `%scope_id` from `client_ip()` but `incidr()` cannot parse it; ACL network parsing rejects zone suffixes and matching discards the peer scope ID. | No |
| §11.1 Non-Global Addresses | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/bind/mod.rs:47-58` | **Partial**: interface expansion adds zones to link-local unicast and scope-2 multicast only, not other non-global multicast scopes such as interface-local, realm-local, admin-local, site-local, or organization-local. It correctly leaves loopback addresses unzoned. | No |
| §11.1 Non-Global Addresses | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/expression/mod.rs:777-788` | **Partial**: `write_ip` appends `%scope_id` to every scoped IPv6 socket address, including global and loopback addresses where RFC 4007 says the form should not be used. | No |
| §11.2 The `<zone_id>` Part | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:402-409` | **Partial**: zone text resolves only through `if_nametoindex`; decimal zone indices, including `%0`, are treated as interface names and are not supported. Omitting the zone yields the default scope ID. | No |
| §11.2 The `<zone_id>` Part | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:45-54` | **Violation**: `primary_matches` accepts interface-name zones but not numeric indices, and its comment cites RFC 4007 §11 rather than §11.2. No plugin README records this deviation, and ADR 0002 requires CoreDNS-compatible differences to be recorded in the compatibility document, which omits it. | No |
| §11.7 Combinations of Delimiter Characters | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/expression/mod.rs:777-819`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/acl/mod.rs:104-120` | **Partial**: `client_ip()` can include a numeric scope ID, but `incidr()` and ACL CIDR parsing use `IpAddr`, which rejects zone-qualified prefixes such as `fe80::%2/64`. Callers cannot disambiguate a non-global prefix by zone as the section recommends, including for Realm-Local multicast added by RFC 7346. | No |
| §12 Security Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/acl/mod.rs:104-120,195-199,237-240` | **Violation**: ACL rules accept unqualified link-local prefixes, then compare only the client's bare `IpAddr`, discarding its IPv6 scope ID. RFC 4007 warns that link-local addresses without a zone index cannot be used in ACLs; no README or ADR justifies this behavior. | No |

## RFC 4033: DNS Security Introduction and Requirements

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3.2 Authenticating Name and Type Non-Existence | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:101-152, 234-289` | **Partial**: The plugin synthesizes and signs NSEC records for negative replies, but it does not check synthetic spans or bitmaps against authoritative zone data. A proof can therefore claim false name or type non-existence; the DNSSEC plugin README records this limitation. | Yes |
| §8.1 TTL Values vs. RRSIG Validity Period | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:570-618`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:163-207` | **Partial**: DO responses with signatures already expired are rejected, but cache lifetime uses RR TTLs only. A signature that expires before its TTL can still be served from cache after its validity period. | No |
| §8.1 TTL Values vs. RRSIG Validity Period | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:618`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:54` | **Violation**: §8.1 says a caching resolver purges RRsets no later than their TTL. `ttl.max(minimum)` raises cache lifetime to `success_min_ttl`, which defaults to 5 s, so RRsets with TTL below 5 s stay past TTL. Neither the cache README nor an ADR records this default. | No |
| §8.2 New Temporal Dependency Issues for Zones | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:83-85` | **Partial**: The signer re-signs the SOA after replacing its serial with the current Unix timestamp. Equal or backward timestamps do not advance the serial as required; no documented exception found. | No |
| §12 Security Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/transfer/mod.rs:222-230`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:84-95` | **Partial**: Transfers can proceed with IP-based authorization alone; TSIG is optional. RFC 4033 §12 requires TSIG, SIG(0), IPsec, or equivalent protection for zone transfers. | No |

## RFC 4034: Resource Records for the DNS Security Extensions

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1.1 The Flags Field | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-359` | **Violation**: The response signer can sign with a DNSKEY whose SEP bit is set while its Zone Key bit is clear. With no recognized KSK or ZSK, the filter leaves that key enabled, although RFC 4034 says the Zone Key bit is needed to generate signatures legally. The comment at `signing.rs:337` names §2.1.1 but does not enforce it. | Yes |
| §2.1.1 The Flags Field | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:288-314`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/setup.rs:88-95`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:93-109`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:1124-1138` | **Violation**: Key loading and generic DNSKEY serialization preserve arbitrary flag bits, so configured keys can emit nonzero reserved bits despite the requirement that they be zero upon creation. | No |
| §2.1.2 The Protocol Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:1124-1138` | **Violation**: Generic DNSKEY serialization emits arbitrary protocol values, although the field MUST be 3. | No |
| §2.2 DNSKEY RR Presentation Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:251-253` | **Violation**: The zone parser accepts any unsigned 8-bit DNSKEY Protocol value, not only 3. The separate key-file parser rejects other values, but this zone path does not; the nearby citation names §2, not §2.2. | No |
| §2.2 DNSKEY RR Presentation Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:252-253`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:294-296` | **Partial**: Both DNSKEY presentation parsers accept decimal Algorithm values but reject valid algorithm mnemonics. The nearby citation names §2, not §2.2. | No |
| §3 The RRSIG Resource Record | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:179-183,215-220` | **Violation**: The offline signer sets apex NS RRSIG TTL to the SOA TTL instead of the covered NS RRset TTL, although RFC 4034 §3 says the RRSIG TTL MUST match the covered RRset TTL. They differ when the source zone gives those RRsets different TTLs; no README or ADR documents this exception. | No |
| §3.1.4 Original TTL Field | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:202-227,257-259,377-380` | **Deviation**: Generated signatures use the lowest TTL received in the response, which can be lower than the covered RRset's authoritative-zone TTL after caching. The dnssec plugin README records this response-signing policy and its CoreDNS compatibility. | Yes |
| §3.1.4 Original TTL Field | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:179-183` | **Partial**: Offline apex NS signatures use the SOA TTL as Original TTL even when the covered NS RRset has a different authoritative TTL. | No |
| §3.1.5 Signature Expiration and Inception Fields | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/log/mod.rs:186-205`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/output.rs:13-46`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/response.rs:99-106`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/response.rs:101-105` | **Violation**: RFC 4034 requires RFC 1982 serial arithmetic for every comparison; log and response validity checks use half-range adjustment, while offline refresh uses ordinary arithmetic, misclassifying wrapped signature times. | Yes |
| §3.1.5 Signature Expiration and Inception Fields | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1179-1189`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_strictness.rs:58-78` | **Violation**: The parser wraps the year-2200 presentation time into a different 32-bit date instead of rejecting a date outside the permitted 68-year range. The comment at `zone/rdata.rs:1179` names §3.1.5 but does not enforce the range. | Yes |
| §3.1.7 The Signer's Name Field | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/setup.rs:72-88`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:97-109,170-220` | **Deviation**: With multiple origins in one `sign` directive, each signature names its zone apex, but shared DNSKEY records use the last origin as owner. Earlier zones therefore lack the DNSKEY owner named by their RRSIG. `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/README.md` documents this CoreDNS-compatible behavior. | Yes |
| §3.1.7 The Signer's Name Field | `xtask/src/dnssec.rs:48-54` | **Partial**: The verifier checks that the signer matches the supplied key owner but does not establish that this name is the zone containing the covered RRset. | No |
| §3.1.8.1 Signature Calculation | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/interop.rs:101-109` | **Partial**: The test verifier selects covered records by owner and type but not class, so unrelated classes can enter the signed RRset and cause valid signatures to fail verification. | No |
| §3.2 RRSIG RR Presentation Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:1436-1458`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/codes.rs:20-25,100` | **Violation**: A Type Covered value of 0 formats as `None`, not RFC 3597's `TYPE0` form; type 0 has no DNS RR mnemonic. No justification recorded. | No |
| §4 NSEC Resource Record | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:151` | **Violation**: generated NSEC TTL is SOA.MINIMUM. RFC 9077 §3.1 updates this to the lesser of SOA.MINIMUM and SOA TTL. | No |
| §4.1.2 Type Bit Maps Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:212-228,874-878,1460-1466` | **Violation**: parsing retains pseudo-type bits and presentation emits them, rather than ignoring them when read. | No |
| §4.1.2 Type Bit Maps Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:305-316`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_strictness.rs:44-47`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_rdata.rs:191` | **Violation**: Zone parsing accepts NSEC RDATA with no type-bitmap blocks, although the field requires one or more nonempty blocks. | Yes |
| §4.1.2 Type Bit Maps Field | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:130-148` | **Violation**: signer includes every RR type at a delegation owner. It does not clear non-NS types for which the parent is not authoritative, as required by §4.1.2. | No |
| §4.1.2 Type Bit Maps Field | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:171-214` | **Deviation**: compact-denial responses use canned bitmap types and omit NXNAME. RFC 9824 §3.1 requires only RRSIG, NSEC, and NXNAME for its nonexistent-name response; the DNSSEC README records the CoreDNS-style behavior. | Yes |
| §6.2 Canonical RR Form | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/canonical.rs:60-63, 293-295` | **Partial**: Canonicalization lowercases supported listed RDATA names and follows RFC 6840 §5.1 for NSEC and RRSIG. A6 and remaining legacy name-bearing types such as MD/MF, MINFO/RP, AFSDB/RT, SIG, and NXT are unsupported, so their RDATA names cannot be canonicalized. | Yes |
| §A.2 DNSSEC Digest Types | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/digest.rs:6-9,54-65` | **Partial**: The signer uses SHA-256 CDS, but public `cds_rdata` still accepts SHA-1 and emits digest type 1. RFC 9904 §4 says SHA-1 MUST NOT be used for DNSSEC delegation. The plugin README `sign/README.md:7` records that only SHA-256 CDS is created. | Yes |

## RFC 4035: Protocol Modifications for the DNS Security Extensions

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1 Including DNSKEY RRs in a Zone — DNSKEY for each signing key | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/setup.rs:71-83`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:93-110`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/README.md:14` | **Partial**: The offline signer publishes each signing key's DNSKEY only at `key_owner`, which setup assigns to the last configured origin. Earlier configured zones therefore lack their signer's DNSKEY; the README documents this CoreDNS-compatible behavior and recommends one directive per origin. | Yes |
| §2.1 Including DNSKEY RRs in a Zone — Zone Key bit | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/setup.rs:79-95`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-359` | **Violation**: The runtime signer accepts keys without the Zone Key bit, publishes their flags, and still uses them to sign response RRsets. Unlike the offline signer, this path does not validate the key flags; RFC 6840 §6.2 clarifies SEP usage but does not remove the Zone Key requirement. | No |
| §2.1 Including DNSKEY RRs in a Zone — apex secure entry point | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/setup.rs:71-83`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:93-110`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/README.md:14` | **Deviation**: With multiple configured origins, earlier zones have no apex DNSKEY, despite the MUST for a signed zone intended to be usable beyond an island of security. The README documents this CoreDNS-compatible behavior; RFC 6840 §6.2 clarifies that the SEP bit itself does not determine secure-entry-point use. | Yes |
| §2.2 Including RRSIG RRs in a Zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-359`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:112-128` | **Violation**: With both KSK and ZSK keys configured, the DNSKEY RRset contains both sets, but DNSKEY signatures use only KSKs and other RRsets use only ZSKs. If those key sets use different algorithms, some RRsets lack a signature for an algorithm in the DNSKEY RRset, contrary to RFC 6840 §5.11. | No |
| §2.2 Including RRSIG RRs in a Zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:59-70,170-208` | **Violation**: When the SOA owner differs from the configured origin, the signer uses the configured origin as the RRSIG Signer's Name, although the RRsets belong to the SOA-owner zone. The test `coredns_signs_a_file_whose_soa_owner_differs_from_origin` asserts the CoreDNS behavior, but no ADR or plugin README records it. | No |
| §2.3 Including NSEC RRs in a Zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:117-129`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:195-214` | **Deviation**: The plugin creates synthetic NSEC spans without checking zone contents. A span can cover existing descendant names, contrary to RFC 4470 §3; the DNSSEC README documents this CoreDNS-compatible limitation. | Yes |
| §2.3 Including NSEC RRs in a Zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:73-85,147-155`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:130-141` | **Violation**: Offline NSEC records use SOA.MINIMUM alone; synthetic online NSEC records use the SOA TTL alone. RFC 9077 §3.2 requires the returned NSEC TTL to be the lesser of SOA.MINIMUM and the SOA TTL. | No |
| §2.3 Including NSEC RRs in a Zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:130-155` | **Violation**: At a delegation owner, the signer puts every input RR type in the NSEC bitmap. It does not clear non-NS types for which the parent is not authoritative, as required. | No |
| §2.4 Including DS RRs in a Zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:130-168`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:453-456` | **Violation**: DS TTLs pass through from zone input and are served unchanged; code does not ensure they match the delegating NS RRset TTL as the RFC SHOULD. | No |
| §2.4 Including DS RRs in a Zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:49-70,130-168`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:153-187` | **Violation**: An input DS RRset at the zone apex is retained and signed. The loader does not reject it, although DS RRsets MUST NOT appear at a zone apex. | No |
| §2.5 Changes to the CNAME Resource Record | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:49-70,130-168`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:199-229` | **Violation**: The signer retains and signs arbitrary RR types at a CNAME owner. RFC 4035 allows only CNAME, its RRSIG and NSEC, and KEY for secure dynamic update at that name. | No |
| §3 Serving — DNAME-synthesized CNAME signatures | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:727-748`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:187-259` | **Violation**: The zone handler emits a synthesized CNAME with a DNAME answer; the DNSSEC plugin then signs every in-zone answer RRset, including that synthesized CNAME. RFC 4035 says servers SHOULD NOT generate its signature, and no exception is documented. | No |
| §3.1.1 Including RRSIG RRs in a Response — Additional-section truncation | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:234-260`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/builder.rs:312-334` | **Violation**: The signer appends Additional-section RRSIGs after all Additional RRsets. If only a signature fails to fit, the builder sets TC; RFC 4035 says not to set TC solely because an Additional-section RRSIG did not fit. | No |
| §3.1.1 Including RRSIG RRs in a Response — Answer priority | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:187-260` | **Violation**: The signer writes all Answer RRsets before generating their RRSIGs. If later Answer data exhausts the message limit, the builder stops before adding signatures, despite the requirement to prioritize them over other RRsets. | No |
| §3.1.1 Including RRSIG RRs in a Response — Authority priority | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:187-260` | **Violation**: The signer writes all Authority RRsets before generating their RRSIGs. If later Authority data exhausts the message limit, the builder stops before adding signatures, despite the requirement to prioritize them over other RRsets. | No |
| §3.1.3 Including NSEC RRs in a Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:101-109`, `crates/pharos-plugins/tests/functional/dnssec_more.rs:680-715` | **Violation**: Denial responses with Authority layouts other than exactly one SOA pass through unsigned and without a generated NSEC proof. The test confirms a No Data response with extra Authority data receives no proof; this can omit required NSEC and RRSIG records. | No |
| §3.1.3.1 Including NSEC RRs: No Data Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:115-117,164-177,262-290` | **Violation**: An NSEC query classified as denial moves the SOA and generated NSEC into Answer. RFC 4035 requires the NSEC proof and its signature in Authority. | No |
| §3.1.3.2 Including NSEC RRs: Name Error Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:99-112,151-156,262-289`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:171-215`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/README.md:17` | **Deviation**: For a DO query with an NXDOMAIN response, the signer clears the RCODE, returns NOERROR, and adds a synthetic NSEC that claims the QNAME exists. RFC 9824's compact-denial alternative uses NXNAME signaling, which this synthetic bitmap omits; the README documents the CoreDNS-style behavior. | Yes |
| §3.1.3.2 Including NSEC RRs: Name Error Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:117-129`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:156-215`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/README.md:17` | **Partial**: The online signer creates a synthetic NSEC without checking whether its range covers existing names. RFC 4470 §3 requires that check; the README documents this limitation. | Yes |
| §3.1.3.2 Including NSEC RRs: Name Error Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:889-929`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/file/tests.rs:432-489` | **Violation**: For a query below an empty non-terminal, the proof search skips that closest encloser and checks the wildcard at a higher ancestor. The returned NSEC therefore does not prove absence of the applicable wildcard. | No |
| §3.1.3.3 Including NSEC RRs: Wildcard Answer Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:750-785,931-948` | **Violation**: For a DNSSEC-enabled wildcard CNAME answer, the code writes the covering RRSIG under the source wildcard owner instead of the expanded QNAME, then skips the Authority NSEC proof of no closer match because the CNAME chain is nonempty. The response therefore lacks the wildcard authentication §3.1.3.3 requires. | No |
| §3.1.3.4 Including NSEC RRs: Wildcard No Data Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:855-867,931-948`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:117-145` | **Partial**: Wildcard NODATA adds the wildcard owner's NSEC and RRSIG, but not the NSEC proof that no closer match exists. RFC 9824 §3.3's alternative requires an NSEC at QNAME with the wildcard's type bitmap; the generated bitmap is synthetic. The shared branch's citation incorrectly names §3.1.3.1. | No |
| §3.1.3.5 Finding the Right NSEC RRs — predecessor selection | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:341-350,875-928` | **Partial**: The lookup selects the canonical predecessor, but searches all tree names and returns no proof when that predecessor has no NSEC (for example, glue below a delegation). It also returns no predecessor when the query sorts before the first tree name. | No |
| §3.1.4 Including DS RRs in a Response — no DS at delegation | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:838-853,950-961` | **Partial**: The referral places NS first and returns DS with matching RRSIGs when DS exists. When DS is absent, this Zone path emits no NSEC proof or RRSIG; the required authenticated denial is not guaranteed without the optional signing plugin. | No |
| §3.1.4.1 Responding to Queries for DS RRs | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/mod.rs:144-151,201-204`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:293-311,518-532,541-544,684-688` | **Violation**: Verified erratum 5226 requires no special child-zone processing when the server is authoritative for any zone above the child's apex, but `Secondary` selects the most-specific child zone for a DS query there. With only apex SOA/NS data, `Zone` can also answer that child-apex DS query with NXDOMAIN instead of authoritative NOERROR/NODATA. | No |
| §3.1.4.1 Responding to Queries for DS RRs | `crates/pharos-server/src/router.rs:160-207`, `crates/pharos-server/tests/router.rs:109-127` | **Violation**: For DS queries, routing keeps the last matching ancestor, which may be above the parent zone. The test follows CoreDNS, but neither ADR 0002's compatibility document nor a plugin README records this choice. | No |
| §3.1.6 The AD and CD Bits in an Authoritative Response — CD bit | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:710-715`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:104-105`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/transfer.rs:59-64`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:161-164`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/chaos/mod.rs:106-109` | **Violation**: These authoritative response paths preserve CD with mask `0x7910` or `0x0110`; RFC 4035 says a security-aware name server SHOULD clear it. The comment at `dnssec/mod.rs:104` cites this section for the opposite behavior; no ADR or README justifies it. | No |
| §4.6 Handling of the CD and AD Bits | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:663-666`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/setup.rs:91` | **Partial**: The cache clears AD only when the query has neither AD nor DO; otherwise it preserves upstream AD. Forward supports no TLS and exposes no AD-trust setting, so it does not disregard the upstream AD bit as §4.6 requires without a secure channel or explicit configuration. | No |

## RFC 4255: Using DNS to Securely Publish Secure Shell (SSH) Key Fingerprints

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3.1 The SSHFP RDATA Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:919-925,1204-1214` | **Deviation**: Wire parsing and encoding accept an SSHFP RDATA containing only the two header octets, with an empty fingerprint. The compatibility document records this CoreDNS-compatible behavior; ADR 0002 permits compatibility where deployments depend on it. | Yes |

## RFC 4291: IP Version 6 Addressing Architecture

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.3 Text Representation of Address Prefixes | `crates/pharos-corefile/src/key.rs:39-44` | **Partial**: ACL, view, DNS64, and SIIT accept valid IPv6 prefixes, and `ipnet::contains` compares their leftmost bits. Server-block prefix normalization rejects valid inputs such as `::1/128` and `0:1::/32` because the guard rejects addresses starting with `:` or `0` followed by `:`. | No |

## RFC 4343: Domain Name System (DNS) Case Insensitivity Clarification

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1 Escaping Unusual DNS Label Octets | `crates/pharos-proto/src/name.rs:115,243-245` | **Partial**: Presentation output escapes unusual octets, but both presentation parsers accept raw non-ASCII bytes. Section 2.1 requires escapes for those octets in ASCII presentation contexts. | Yes |
| §3 Name Lookup, Label Types, and CLASS | `crates/pharos-corefile/src/key.rs:67-68` | **Violation**: `go_lowercase` (`key.rs:74-88`) falls back to `char::to_lowercase`, folding non-ASCII characters in configured names, although DNS case matching folds only ASCII A–Z/a–z and requires other octets to match exactly. | No |
| §3 Name Lookup, Label Types, and CLASS | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:769-772,2196-2199` | **Violation**: CNAME matching passes the presented RDATA name (case kept by `NameRef` Display, `name.rs:417-419`) to `matcher.matches` (`mod.rs:771`) unfolded, while rule text is lowercased at parse (`mod.rs:303-305,354-368`), so mixed-case DNS-equivalent targets miss. No ADR or README records this. | No |
| §3 Name Lookup, Label Types, and CLASS | `xtask/src/compare.rs:49-68,216-217` | **Partial**: Question and owner names compare as case-sensitive strings, so DNS-equivalent responses can be reported as different. This affects comparison results, not server output. | No |
| §3.1 Original DNS Label Types — ASCII case comparison | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/mod.rs:489-490` | **Violation**: This path compares DNS labels byte-for-byte, so a case-only difference prevents correlating the captured target. Its §4.1 citation concerns output preservation, not name comparison, and no document justifies this behavior. | No |
| §3.1 Original DNS Label Types — ASCII case comparison | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/loadbalance/weighted.rs:187-190` | **Deviation**: Weight selection compares DNS labels byte-for-byte. The loadbalance README (line 14) records that CoreDNS compares presentation strings exactly; ADR 0002 permits that CoreDNS-compatible behavior. The README does not name case sensitivity explicitly. | Yes |
| §3.1 Original DNS Label Types — ASCII case comparison | `xtask/src/compare.rs:216-217,283` | **Partial**: Comparison treats owner-name and typed RDATA presentation strings as case-sensitive, so DNS-equivalent responses can be reported as different. This affects comparison results, not server output. | No |
| §4.1 DNS Output Case Preservation | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:983-985,1195-1200` | **Violation**: Rewrite lowercases query and mapped names before response construction, losing their prior ASCII case. | No |
| §4.2 DNS Input Case Preservation | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:140-151` | **Partial**: Zone construction canonicalizes every non-SRV owner before indexing, so new nodes lose input capitalization; SRV owners bypass this conversion. No ADR or plugin README justifies the difference. | No |

## RFC 4398: Storing Certificates in the Domain Name System (DNS)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2 The CERT Resource Record | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/mod.rs:235-247` | **Violation**: Dynamic UPDATE expands short CERT RDATA by zero-padding it to the five-octet header, so malformed input is stored as different RDATA. No RFC or repository exception documents this behavior. | No |
| §2.1 Certificate Type Values | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:107-119` | **Partial**: the parser maps the defined CERT mnemonics, but spells `IPKIX` as `IPIX`; valid `IPKIX` master-file records fail while `IPIX` is accepted. | No |

## RFC 4470: Minimally Covering NSEC Records and DNSSEC On-line Signing

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3 Minimally Covering NSEC Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:195-214`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:117-156` | **Deviation**: RFC requires the generated owner to sort before QNAME and the next name after it. `black_lie` uses QNAME as owner and only generates a following name; the signer also changes NXDOMAIN to NOERROR. The plugin README records this CoreDNS-compatible behavior. Verified erratum 81 corrects the escaped literals in the example physically located here; it does not change the ordering rule. | Yes |
| §3 Minimally Covering NSEC Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:159-215`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:117-129` | **Deviation**: Generated spans must not cover existing names. The plugin has no authoritative name inventory to check spans; its README notes that referral spans can cover descendants. | Yes |
| §3 Minimally Covering NSEC Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:159-215`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:117-129` | **Deviation**: Before answering, RFC requires checking the span and using a better epsilon or the closest covered existing name when needed. The plugin cannot perform this fallback because it lacks the zone inventory. | Yes |
| §3 Minimally Covering NSEC Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:159-214`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:99-145,262-289` | **Violation**: For NODATA, QNAME exists and `black_lie` uses it as the synthetic NSEC owner. The signer returns that synthetic record instead of the existing name's real NSEC. | No |
| §3 Minimally Covering NSEC Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:171-195` | **Partial**: For a negative reply to QTYPE RRSIG, `types.retain` (line 193) removes the RRSIG bit, breaking the MUST; otherwise the bitmap has the required RRSIG and NSEC bits but also lists many other types despite the RFC saying it SHOULD NOT. | No |
| §3 Minimally Covering NSEC Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:262-282,350-367` | **Violation**: Generated NSEC records need signatures for every algorithm in the zone DNSKEY RRset. Special NSEC replies use `SignMode::Last`, so only the last configured key signs the record. Outside those replies, when both KSK and ZSK exist the filter (lines 350-359) keeps only ZSKs, so an algorithm with only a KSK gets no RRSIG. | No |
| §4 Better Epsilon Functions | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:159-215`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:117-129` | **Deviation**: An authoritative server should ensure generated spans cover no existing names. The plugin does not check spans against zone data; its README records this limitation. | Yes |

## RFC 4509: Use of SHA-256 in DNSSEC Delegation Signer (DS) Resource Records (RRs)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.2 DS Record with SHA-256 Wire Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:815-834, 1108-1122`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:234-243` | **Partial**: DS fields are parsed and written in the specified order, but the parser and writer accept any digest length when digest type is 2. A short SHA-256 digest can therefore be loaded and emitted instead of the required 32-byte value. | No |

## RFC 4592: The Role of Wildcards in the Domain Name System

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.2.1 An Example | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/hosts/data.rs:138-149,184-187` | **Deviation**: `hosts` replaces only the first QNAME label when seeking a wildcard, so `*.example.org` does not answer `a.b.example.org` when `b.example.org` is absent, unlike the RFC example. The CoreDNS-port test preserves this behavior; ADR 0002 permits CoreDNS-compatible behavior, but this specific difference is not recorded in the plugin README. | No |
| §2.2.3 Yet Another Definition of Existence | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:200-224,541-544,684-687` | **Violation**: apex SOA/NS records do not mark the apex as existing, and the exact-name exception recognizes the apex only for SOA or NS queries. In a zone containing only apex SOA and NS records, an apex A query therefore returns NXDOMAIN instead of NOERROR with no answer. The cite at `zone/mod.rs:220` restates the code's rule, not §2.2.3's zone-boundary note, so it does not count as documentation. | No |
| §3.2 Step 3 | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:541-581` | **Violation**: `exact` means the node owns an RRSet, not that the name exists. An existing empty non-terminal therefore enters the wildcard branch and can receive synthesized data; the nearby citation names §3.3.1, not §3.2. | No |
| §3.3 Part 'c' | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/hosts/data.rs:133-149` | **Partial**: wildcard lookup checks only `*.<immediate parent>`. For a missing intermediate name, it does not try the wildcard below the closest existing encloser, so valid synthesis can be missed. | No |
| §3.3.1 Closest Encloser and the Source of Synthesis | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:541-581` | **Violation**: an existing empty non-terminal QNAME is not treated as the closest encloser because synthesis is gated by `exact`, which excludes empty non-terminals. The lookup can synthesize from the QNAME's parent instead of terminating on the existing name. | Yes |
| §3.3.1 Closest Encloser and the Source of Synthesis | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/hosts/data.rs:133-149` | **Partial**: the hosts lookup never searches ancestor suffixes for the closest encloser. It misses synthesis from `*.<closest encloser>` whenever the immediate parent does not exist. | No |
| §4.4 DNAME RRSet at a Wildcard Domain Name | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:166-180,563-581,794-810` | **Violation**: Zone loading does not reject wildcard-owned DNAME, and wildcard lookup can synthesize its RRSet with QNAME as owner. RFC says wildcard-owned DNAME is to be avoided or rejected; no focused README or ADR records why Pharos accepts it. | No |
| §4.7 NSEC RRSet at a Wildcard Domain Name | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:130-155` | **Partial**: the signer creates NSEC records only for owners that already have records. An empty non-terminal wildcard owner is omitted from the generated NSEC chain. | No |
| §4.7 NSEC RRSet at a Wildcard Domain Name | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:794-810` | **Violation**: when QTYPE is NSEC, this path can copy the wildcard source's NSEC RRset to a synthesized QNAME. RFC 4592 §4.7 permits NSEC synthesis only when the query exactly matches the record. Erratum 5119 is `Reported`, not normative. | No |

## RFC 4648: The Base16, Base32, and Base64 Data Encodings

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |

Oversized configuration data can therefore amplify memory use before rejection, although the inspected Rust paths avoid buffer-overflow-style indexing. | No |
