## RFC 5155: DNS Security (DNSSEC) Hashed Authenticated Denial of Existence

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3.1.2 Flags | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:882-907,1167-1180` | **Violation**: NSEC3 parsing and serialization accept undefined flag bits, although RFC 5155 requires them to be zero. | No |
| §3.1.3 Iterations (RFC 9276 §3.1) | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:294-322` | **Violation**: `nsec3_chain` accepts and hashes nonzero iteration counts, despite RFC 9276 §3.1 requiring publishers to use 0 when they use NSEC3. | No |
| §3.1.6 Hash Length | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:898-907, 1175-1180` | **Violation**: Parsing and serialization accept a zero-length Next Hashed Owner Name. RFC 5155 requires 1–255 octets; the one-octet length only enforces the upper bound. | No |
| §3.2.1 Type Bit Maps Encoding | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:1175-1180`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1004-1022` | **Partial**: The chain builder rejects type 0, but raw NSEC3 RDATA parsing and serialization (`valid_bitmap`) and master-file encoding accept bit 0 in window 0. | No |
| §3.2.1 Type Bit Maps Encoding | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:44-50`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:1175-1180`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1004-1022` | **Partial**: The chain builder rejects meta-types and QTYPEs except type 128, but generic bitmap encoding permits all such types. RFC 6895 §3.1 reserves 128–255 for QTYPEs and Meta-TYPEs; RFC 6840 §6.4 permits an empty bitmap for an empty non-terminal, which the builder and wire parser preserve. | Yes |
| §4 The NSEC3PARAM Resource Record | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:294-299` | **Partial**: RFC 9276 §3.1 requires publishers using NSEC3 to use zero iterations, but this library chain builder accepts nonzero values and can construct noncompliant signing data. No runtime caller publishes its output, so this finding concerns library construction, not live zone publishing. | No |
| §4.1.3 Iterations | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:294-299,322-335` | **Violation**: RFC 9276 §3.1 requires NSEC3 publishers to use iteration count 0. The NSEC3 chain generator accepts any `u16` and emits that value; no README or ADR justifies this. | No |
| §4.1.5 Salt | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:294-299,322-335` | **Violation**: RFC 9276 §3.1 says operators SHOULD NOT use a salt and recommends zero-length salt. The NSEC3 chain generator accepts and emits nonempty salts, with no documented justification. | No |
| §7.1 Zone Signing | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:290-347`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:139-155`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:115-117,159-160` | **Partial**: `nsec3_chain` builds a circular chain for supplied owners and empty non-terminals, but the zone signer never calls it and rejects NSEC3 zones. The helper does not provide an end-to-end NSEC3 signing path. | No |
| §7.1 Zone Signing | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:294-299,322-336` | **Violation**: RFC 9276 §3.1 requires publishers using NSEC3 to use zero iterations. `nsec3_chain` accepts any `u16` and copies it into every generated record. | No |
| §7.1 Zone Signing | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:294-299,322-336` | **Violation**: RFC 9276 §3.1 says operators SHOULD NOT use a salt and recommends a zero-length value. `nsec3_chain` accepts and emits non-empty salts without a documented reason. | No |
| §9.2 Use of the AD Bit | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:378-397` | **Violation**: Forward copies upstream AD unchanged, including when a response contains an Opt-Out closest-encloser proof. The RFC 6840 §5.8 citation in cache code covers query signaling, not this prohibition. | No |
| §10.3 Iterations — RFC 9276 update | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:294-299,322-337` | **Violation**: RFC 9276 §3.1 requires publishers to use zero iterations when using NSEC3. The builder accepts nonzero values, including 12 in `crates/pharos-dnssec/tests/denial.rs:189-194`. | No |
| §10.3 Iterations — per-key ceiling | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:294-299,322-337` | **Violation**: `nsec3_chain` accepts any `u16` iteration count and has no key-size input or check for RFC 5155's ceiling table. Callers can generate chains above the limit for their signing-key size. | No |
| §11 IANA Considerations | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:882-896,1182-1190` | **Violation**: NSEC3PARAM parsing and serialization accept any flags byte. Section 11 reserves bit 7 and requires it to be zero, but this code preserves a set bit. | No |
| §12.1.1 Dictionary Attacks | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:294-301` | **Partial**: RFC 9276 §3.1 supersedes the earlier salt advice and recommends zero-length salt. `nsec3_chain` takes caller-supplied salt and permits nonempty values, but has no default or policy to guide the signer toward zero length. | No |
| §12.1.4 Using High Iteration Values | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:219-243,294-299` | **Violation**: `nsec3_chain` accepts any `u16` iteration count and `nsec3_hash` performs that many additional rounds. RFC 9276 §3.1 requires zone publishers to use 0 iterations when using NSEC3. | No |
| §C.2.1 Avoiding Hash Collisions During Generation | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:339-342` | **Partial**: `nsec3_chain` detects a duplicate hash and returns `HashCollision`, whose message tells the caller to choose another salt. As the chain generator/signer, it does not choose that salt or retry to regenerate the full chain; the nearby citation names §7.1, not §C.2.1. | No |

## RFC 5452: Measures for Making DNS More Resilient against Forged Answers

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3 Description of DNS Spoofing — matching the question section | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:560-567`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/resolve.rs:84-104` | **Partial**: The proxy compares response QNAME, QTYPE, and QCLASS with the query. `forward::resolve::lookup` checks only the ID before consuming A/AAAA answers, so a response with a matching ID but a different question can seed configured hostname resolution. | No |
| §4.2 Matching the Question Section | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:560-568`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/resolve.rs:84-89`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/health.rs:230-235` | **Partial**: Normal forwarding checks the response Question section against the query. Setup-time hostname resolution and upstream health checks accept responses without checking that Question section. | No |
| §4.2 Matching the Question Section | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:183-216` | **Partial**: The secondary accepts an SOA from any response with the matching ID and RCODE; it does not match the echoed Question section to the SOA query. | No |
| §6 Accepting Only In-Domain Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:596-598`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:302-304`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/resolve.rs:89-103` | **Violation**: The cache stores every Answer, Authority, and Additional record of a forwarded Response with no owner-name check, and setup lookups take A/AAAA data from any owner name. No ADR or plugin README justifies this, and ADR 0002 defaults to RFC behaviour. | No |
| §9.1 Query Matching Rules | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:507-567`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/resolve.rs:84-104` | **Partial**: The forwarding proxy checks the response ID and all Question fields. Setup-time A/AAAA lookups check only the ID, then accept matching-type answer data without checking the response Question name, class, or type. | Yes |
| §9.1 Query Matching Rules | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/loop/mod.rs:188-193` | **Partial**: The loop probe accepts any parseable response with the matching ID; it does not match the response QNAME, QTYPE, or QCLASS to its query. | No |
| §9.2 Avoiding Certain Ports | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:339-342`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:160-172` | **Missing**: UDP binds use port 0, and forward configuration exposes no way to exclude specific source ports. | No |
| §9.2 Unpredictable Source Addresses | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:160-172`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:350-364` | **Missing**: A proxy can bind one configured source address or use the route-selected address; it does not choose unpredictably among multiple local addresses. | No |
| §9.2 Unpredictable Source Port | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:339-342,447-475,572-585` | **Partial**: New UDP sockets bind port 0, but the proxy reuses idle UDP sockets, so an observed source port predicts later queries on that socket. Port unpredictability for new sockets is delegated to the OS. | No |
| §9.2.1 Justification and Discussion | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:339-342,447-475,572-585` | **Violation**: Reused UDP sockets keep a source port constant across later queries, and source-address selection is fixed by configuration or routing. This leaves predictable endpoint state despite random query IDs; no ADR or plugin README justifies it. | No |

## RFC 5625: DNS Proxy Implementation Guidelines

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3 The Transparency Principle | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:182-185`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:407-414` | **Partial**: the proxy normally uses the client's transport, but `prefer_udp` sends TCP-client queries upstream over UDP. The forward README records this CoreDNS-compatible option. | Yes |
| §4.4.1 TCP Transport | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:407-414` | **Deviation**: With `prefer_udp`, the proxy sends even a TCP-received Query over UDP first, then retries over TCP only if the UDP response has TC. The `forward` README documents this CoreDNS-compatible transport preference. | Yes |
| §4.4.2 Extension Mechanisms for DNS (EDNS0) | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:57-64`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/edns.rs:26-33` | **Partial**: OPT-bearing Queries with EDNS version 0 are proxied, but the Listener answers an unsupported EDNS version locally with BADVERS instead of proxying it. RFC 5625 §4.4.2 forbids refusing OPT-bearing Messages; RFC 6891 §6.1.3 requires the BADVERS response. No ADR or README records the choice. | No |
| §4.5 Secret Key Transaction Authentication for DNS (TSIG) | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:166-178`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:253-265`, `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:108-147,327-329` | **Violation**: RFC 5625 §4.5 requires RFC 2845 §4.7, replaced by RFC 8945 §5.5. For a matching configured key, `tsig` authenticates the incoming query and removes its TSIG before `forward` sends it upstream; the Listener then signs the upstream response. The forwarder must clear AD before adding its TSIG when no transaction security reaches the upstream and the response has AD; response finalization in `outcome.rs` never clears AD. | No |
| §6.1 Forgery Resilience | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:476-487` | **Deviation**: Upstream queries get unpredictable random IDs (RFC 5452 §9.2), but TSIG-signed Queries with unshared keys keep the client's ID, because RFC 8945 §5.5 requires forwarding them unchanged. The `forward` README documents that TSIG Messages retain their original ID. | Yes |
| §6.2 Interface Binding | `crates/pharos-corefile/src/setup.rs:61-65`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:399-402`, `crates/pharos-server/src/lib.rs:877-899`, `crates/pharos/src/app.rs:41-43` | **Violation**: The default listener address is unspecified and binds a dual-stack wildcard socket, so a deployment using `forward` can expose the DNS proxy on WAN interfaces. The `bind` directive allows manual restriction, but no default WAN exclusion or justification appears in the bind README or ADRs. | No |
| §6.3 Packet Filtering | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:24-47` | **Partial**: Malformed Messages with complete headers receive a header-only FORMERR, but DNS packets shorter than 12 octets are dropped. RFC 5625 recommends an immediate DNS error response instead of dropping malformed packets. | No |

## RFC 5702: Use of SHA-2 Algorithms with RSA in DNSKEY and RRSIG Resource Records for DNSSEC

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1 RSA/SHA-256 DNSKEY Resource Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/sign.rs:75-85`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:129-149` | **Violation**: RFC 5702 §2.1 says RSA/SHA-256 keys MUST NOT be more than 4096 bits, but `from_pkcs8` and the key loader accept 2048–8192-bit RSA keys (aws-lc-rs range, sign.rs:79). The 2048-bit floor is stricter than RFC 5702's 512 and is recorded in `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/README.md`; the 8192-bit ceiling is not. | No |
| §2.2 RSA/SHA-512 DNSKEY Resource Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/sign.rs:75-85`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:129-149` | **Violation**: RFC 5702 §2.2 says RSA/SHA-512 keys MUST NOT be more than 4096 bits, but the same loader accepts 2048–8192-bit keys. The 2048-bit floor is stricter than the RFC's 1024 and is recorded in `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/README.md`; the 8192-bit ceiling is not. | No |

## RFC 5936: DNS Zone Transfer Protocol (AXFR)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.2 AXFR Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/erratic/mod.rs:45-51`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/erratic/xfr.rs:59-99` | **Deviation**: the `erratic` test plugin can end a successful-looking AXFR without its closing SOA to simulate truncation; its README records this scenario. | Yes |
| §2.2 AXFR Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/transfer.rs:31-34,66-73,123-131`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/transfer.rs:136-168,484-520`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/transfer.rs:128-133,696-707`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/erratic/xfr.rs:20-24,53-93` | **Violation**: these builders echo the requested QCLASS but never match it to the Zone; the zone stream emits stored record classes, while the kubernetes, k8s_external and erratic builders hard-code class IN, so a non-IN transfer returns records from a different class. | No |
| §2.2 AXFR Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:92-127`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:194-198` | **Violation**: the AXFR client retains duplicate non-SOA RRs instead of ignoring duplicates as required. The comment at `update.rs:95` cites §2.2 for name expansion, which is §3.4; the §2.2 citation there is wrong. | No |
| §2.2 AXFR Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:194-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/transfer.rs:83-96`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/transfer.rs:245-260` | **Violation**: duplicate records survive Zone construction and are emitted; the external Kubernetes builder also emits an address once per port. | No |
| §2.2 AXFR Response | `xtask/src/differential.rs:499-516`, `xtask/src/compare.rs:90-117` | **Violation**: the differential AXFR client preserves duplicate RRs, so semantically equivalent transfers can compare as different. | No |
| §2.2 AXFR Response | `xtask/src/differential.rs:487-516` | **Partial**: the differential client flattens all response Messages into one DNS Message, so valid transfers with more than 65,535 combined octets fail comparison. | No |
| §2.2 AXFR Response | `xtask/src/differential.rs:453-468` | **Partial**: the differential client permits an empty first response and does not verify that the opening SOA belongs to the requested zone. | No |
| §2.2.1 Header Values | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/transfer.rs:59-64`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/transfer.rs:155-161`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/transfer.rs:120-126`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/erratic/xfr.rs:21-24`, `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:77-92` | **Violation**: the `0x0110` response mask copies CD (`0x0010`) from the query; RFC 5936 requires response CD to be zero. | No |
| §2.2.2 Question Section | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/transfer.rs:66-73` | **Partial**: the regular Zone stream rebuilds the first Question name from the stored apex instead of copying the query name; it preserves QTYPE and QCLASS. | No |
| §2.2.5 Additional Section | `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:55-65`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/transfer/mod.rs:26-35`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/transfer.rs:57-73`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/transfer.rs:155-172`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/transfer.rs:120-137` | **Violation**: transfer requests discard OPT and streamed responses emit none; RFC 5936 §2.2.5 recommends OPT in the first response, and RFC 9103 §6.3.4 requires it in every response when requested. | No |
| §2.3 TCP Connection Aborts | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:171-175,303-315` | **Partial**: the client ends the failed session but does not try to distinguish network closure from server policy before retrying. | No |
| §2.3 TCP Connection Aborts | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:303-315` | **Violation**: startup transfer failures retry forever; the delay caps at 10 seconds, but the RFC says clients should avoid endless retry cycles. | No |
| §2.3 TCP Connection Aborts | `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:235-247` | **Violation**: any transfer producer error closes the shared TCP connection, even when unrelated sessions may be outstanding; RFC guidance reserves this action for suspected abusive clients. | No |
| §3.1 Records to Include | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:145-160`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/transfer.rs:74-136` | **Partial**: Zone construction accepts owners outside the apex and rejects NSEC3/NSEC3PARAM zones, so transfers can include non-zone records and cannot carry all supported zone contents. | No |
| §3.1 Records to Include | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/transfer.rs:368-477` | **Partial**: the generated AXFR snapshot omits served records such as `dns-version` TXT and ClusterIP/endpoint PTR records, so it cannot reconstruct the DNS-visible zone. | No |
| §3.4 Name Compression | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:145-174`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/builder.rs:356-365` | **Violation**: Zone construction lowercases owner and selected RDATA names, so AXFR emits labels in lowercase and loses configured label-case distinctions; builder compression is case-sensitive and does not cause the loss. | No |
| §4.1.1 AXFR Client TCP — connection reuse | `crates/pharos-server/src/transfer.rs:165-183`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:75-93,178-200` | **Violation**: the secondary closes the SOA-check connection and opens another for AXFR; neither path reuses an open connection or documents a listed exception. | No |
| §4.1.2 AXFR Server TCP — remote close | `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:185-190,310` | **Violation**: remote closure ends the read loop but does not cancel active transfer tasks; they can continue until a later write fails or the stream ends. | No |
| §5 Authorization | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/transfer/mod.rs:282-299` | **Partial**: `to` accepts exact IP addresses and `*`, but not IP address ranges as RFC 5936 recommends. | No |
| §5 Authorization | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/transfer/mod.rs:211-228`, `crates/pharos-plugins/tests/functional/transfer.rs:121-151` | **Deviation**: exact-text ACL matching preserves CoreDNS behavior under ADR 0002; equivalent IPv6 spellings can be denied. | Yes |
| §6 Zone Integrity | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:304-315,334-345` | **Partial**: failed transfers preserve the previous active Zone, but the client does not remember the rejected transfer's SOA serial as recommended. | No |
| §6 Zone Integrity | `crates/pharos-server/src/transfer.rs:195-219`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:192-197`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:135-154` | **Violation**: the `axfr_coredns` path accepts a mismatched final SOA, so an incomplete transfer can replace the active snapshot. ADR 0002 keeps RFC MUSTs unless deployments rely on CoreDNS, and no evidence or compatibility-document entry records that here. | No |
| §6.3.1 Connection Reuse | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:75-92,178-200`, `crates/pharos-server/src/transfer.rs:165-183` | **Violation**: the secondary checks SOA and transfers over separate new connections instead of reusing an open connection to the same primary; no listed exception is documented. | No |
| §6.3.4 The edns-tcp-keepalive EDNS(0) Option | `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:55-65`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/transfer/mod.rs:26-35`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/transfer.rs:57-73`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/transfer.rs:155-172`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/transfer.rs:120-137` | **Violation**: streamed AXFR responses omit OPT even when the query contains it; RFC 9103 requires OPT in every response. | No |

## RFC 6052: IPv6 Addressing of IPv4/IPv6 Translators

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.2 IPv4-Embedded IPv6 Address Format — prefix lengths | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/siit/mod.rs:50-60`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:91-97` | **Violation**: Both parsers accept `64:ff9b::` with prefix lengths other than /96. RFC 6052 defines that Well-Known Prefix only as /96, and only in the /96 format. | No |
| §2.2 IPv4-Embedded IPv6 Address Format — reserved octet | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:81-97,180-189` | **Partial**: SIIT rejects a nonzero reserved octet, but DNS64 accepts a /96 prefix with nonzero bits 64–71 and emits them unchanged. | No |
| §3.1 Restrictions on the Use of the Well-Known Prefix — MUST NOT represent non-global IPv4 | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/siit/mod.rs:30,102-126`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:31,177-199` | **Violation**: Both use the Well-Known Prefix by default without checking whether the embedded IPv4 address is global. SIIT extracts non-global addresses, and DNS64 synthesizes them. No ADR or plugin README justifies this. | No |
| §3.1 Restrictions on the Use of the Well-Known Prefix — MUST NOT translate and MUST drop non-global IPv4 | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/siit/mod.rs:102-126` | **Violation**: `translate` returns the IPv4 address for a Well-Known Prefix address with a non-global IPv4 instead of dropping it. No ADR or plugin README justifies this. | No |
| §3.3 Choice of Prefix for Stateless Translation Deployments | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/siit/mod.rs:29-33` | **Partial**: SIIT permits a Network-Specific Prefix, but defaults to the Well-Known Prefix. RFC 6052 recommends a Network-Specific Prefix for stateless deployments; no ADR or plugin README records a rationale for this default. | No |

## RFC 6147: DNS64: DNS Extensions for Network Address Translation from IPv6 Clients to IPv4 Servers

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §5 DNS64 Normative Specification | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:19-25,30-35,180-189` | **Partial**: DNS64 accepts one synthesis prefix, but cannot map separate IPv4 ranges to separate IPv6 prefixes as §5 recommends. | No |
| §5 DNS64 Normative Specification | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:100-112` | **Missing**: DNS64 does not answer PTR queries for addresses containing its synthesis prefixes; its handler intercepts only AAAA queries. | No |
| §5.1.2 The Answer when There is an Error | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:127-130`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/response.rs:52-67,69-76` | **Violation**: With DO set, an RCODE=3 response containing an expired RRSIG is classified as `OtherError`, so DNS64 attempts synthesis instead of returning the Name Error under normal DNS handling. | No |
| §5.1.3 Dealing with Timeouts | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:115-117`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:400` | **Partial**: A timed-out AAAA lookup becomes SERVFAIL and DNS64 returns it at once. The timeout is treated as RCODE=2, but §5.1.2 requires an RCODE other than 0 or 3 to be treated as an empty answer, so no A lookup or synthesis follows. | No |
| §5.1.4 Special Exclusion Set for AAAA Records — answer contains both excluded and usable AAAA | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:127-130` | **Violation**: By default, the response is returned unchanged instead of retaining only usable AAAA records or treating the answer as empty. | No |
| §5.1.4 Special Exclusion Set for AAAA Records — answer contains only excluded AAAA | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:127-130` | **Violation**: An AAAA answer is never treated as empty based on its addresses, so DNS64 does not proceed to synthesis for an excluded-only answer. | No |
| §5.1.4 Special Exclusion Set for AAAA Records — configure exclusion ranges | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:19-35,100-112` | **Violation**: DNS64 config has one synthesis prefix but no exclusion-range set. Operators cannot mark unusable AAAA ranges as empty answers. | No |
| §5.1.4 Special Exclusion Set for AAAA Records — default `::ffff/96` exclusion | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:127-130` | **Violation**: No default exclusion is configured for the RFC's `::ffff/96` range; the default path returns AAAA answers unchanged. | No |
| §5.1.4 Special Exclusion Set for AAAA Records — never return excluded AAAA | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:127-130` | **Violation**: The unchanged-response path can return excluded AAAA records to the client. | No |
| §5.1.5 Dealing with CNAME and DNAME — follow chain to terminal A or AAAA | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:138-147,171-208` | **Partial**: The plugin issues one A lookup at the original name and preserves returned aliases, but does not itself continue when that lookup returns only a CNAME/DNAME chain without a terminal record. | No |
| §5.1.7 Performing the Synthesis — select prefix by configured IPv4 ranges | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:28-35,177-189` | **Partial**: The plugin synthesizes each A record with one configured prefix, but has no IPv4-range mapping or per-record prefix selection. | No |
| §5.2 Generation of the IPv6 Representations of IPv4 Addresses | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:81-97,180-189`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/siit/mod.rs:47-60,102-126` | **Violation**: DNS64 accepts a configured /96 prefix with non-zero bits 64–71 and synthesizes addresses with it. RFC 6052 requires those bits to be zero for a /96 Network-Specific Prefix; SIIT rejects the same prefix. | No |
| §5.3.1 PTR Resource Record — address matching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:100-114` | **Missing**: The handler intercepts only AAAA queries; no path decodes IP6.ARPA PTR names or checks their addresses against synthesis prefixes. | No |
| §5.3.1 PTR Resource Record — response strategy | — | **Missing**: DNS64 provides neither authoritative PTR handling for matching prefixes nor the synthesized CNAME to IN-ADDR.ARPA required by one of the two alternatives. | No |
| §5.3.2 Handling the Additional Section | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:171-207` | **Partial**: the code restricts synthesis to A records in Answer, but re-parses and re-serializes all other records. Unsupported name-bearing or malformed records return an error instead of passing through unchanged; the plugin README records this limitation. | Yes |
| §5.3.3 Other Resource Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:107-112,200-207` | **Partial**: non-AAAA queries, including A queries, pass through, and parsed records are copied semantically. Unsupported name-bearing or malformed records in a synthesized response fail instead of being returned unchanged; the plugin README records this limitation. | Yes |
| §5.4 Assembling a Synthesized Response to a AAAA Query | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:157-166` | **Violation**: The response flags keep only TC and RCODE from the A lookup, plus selected query flags; they drop AA and RA. RFC 6147 requires header fields to follow the normal rules for the server's authoritative or recursive role. | No |
| §5.4 Assembling a Synthesized Response to a AAAA Query | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dns64/mod.rs:127-147,166-209` | **Violation**: When the A self-lookup returns an empty answer, the plugin still builds a response from that A response. It does not return the response received for the original AAAA query, as §5.4 requires. | No |

## RFC 6303: Locally Served DNS Zones

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §1 Introduction | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:78-90` | **Partial**: The local plugin recognizes `0.in-addr.arpa`, `127.in-addr.arpa`, and `255.in-addr.arpa`; it omits the RFC 1918 and listed IPv6 zones. The listed broadcast zone is `255.255.255.255.in-addr.arpa`, not the broader `255.in-addr.arpa`. | No |
| §3 Changes to Iterative Resolver Behaviour | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:111-165` | **Partial**: For intercepted reverse names, code returns NXDOMAIN/SOA outside recognized records and SOA/NS or NODATA at apexes, but omits AA=1. Zone-list coverage appears under §1 and §4. | No |
| §3 Changes to Iterative Resolver Behaviour | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:20-47` | **Violation**: The local plugin has no zone-by-zone enable/disable setting; `localhost_prefix` controls only localhost matching. | No |
| §3 Changes to Iterative Resolver Behaviour | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:175` | **Violation**: The zone-apex NS target is `localhost`, not the zone name as recommended. | No |
| §3 Changes to Iterative Resolver Behaviour | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:16-18` | **Violation**: SOA RNAME is `root.localhost`, not the recommended `nobody.invalid.`. | No |
| §3 Changes to Iterative Resolver Behaviour | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:27-43` | **Violation**: Operators cannot set the NS or SOA values; the only local property is `localhost_prefix`. | No |
| §4.1 RFC 1918 Zones | — | **Missing**: The local plugin does not serve the listed RFC 1918 reverse zones: `10.IN-ADDR.ARPA`, `16`–`31.172.IN-ADDR.ARPA`, and `168.192.IN-ADDR.ARPA`. | No |
| §4.2 RFC 5735 and RFC 5737 Zones | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:78-90` | **Partial**: It serves `0.IN-ADDR.ARPA` and `127.IN-ADDR.ARPA`, but omits `254.169.IN-ADDR.ARPA`, TEST-NET-1/2/3, and the listed `255.255.255.255.IN-ADDR.ARPA`; the `255` match instead selects `255.IN-ADDR.ARPA`. | Yes |
| §4.3 Local IPv6 Unicast Addresses | — | **Missing**: The local plugin does not serve the IPv6 unspecified or loopback reverse zones. | No |
| §4.4 IPv6 Locally Assigned Local Addresses | — | **Missing**: The local plugin does not serve `D.F.IP6.ARPA`. | No |
| §4.5 IPv6 Link-Local Addresses | — | **Missing**: The local plugin does not serve the four listed link-local reverse zones (`8.E.F` through `B.E.F.IP6.ARPA`). | No |
| §4.6 IPv6 Example Prefix | — | **Missing**: The local plugin does not serve `8.B.D.0.1.0.0.2.IP6.ARPA`. | No |

## RFC 6604: xNAME RCODE and Status Bits Clarification

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1 The Authoritative Answer Bit | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/header/mod.rs:61-72,84-90`, `crates/pharos-plugins/tests/functional/header.rs:22,66` | **Violation**: `header response set aa` or `clear aa` overrides AA without checking authority for the first answer owner. The override is CoreDNS-compatible, but the header plugin README does not mention it and ADR 0002 only states the general CoreDNS-match policy, so no recorded justification exists. | No |
| §3 RCODE Clarification | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:676-683`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/mod.rs:671-700`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:1582-1588,1619-1625`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:817-829,850-863,1098-1115`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/mod.rs:198-205,795-808,497-528` | **Violation**: The zone plugin maps target RCODEs other than SERVFAIL and NXDOMAIN to NOERROR. Template, CNAME rewrite, Kubernetes ExternalName, and k8s_external service-to-target chasing keep the initial RCODE instead of the target lookup's final RCODE. | No |

## RFC 6605: Elliptic Curve Digital Signature Algorithm (DSA) for DNSSEC

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §4 DNSKEY and RRSIG Resource Records for ECDSA | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:150-160`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/sign.rs:56-68,107-121`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:198-221` | **Partial**: Pharos signs with both ECDSA algorithms and emits fixed-width `r\|\|s`, but has no production ECDSA signature-verification path. Verification appears only in tests and the `xtask` dev checker (`xtask/src/dnssec.rs`, P-256 only); RFC requires implementations that create DNS records to implement both signing and verification for both algorithms. | No |

## RFC 6672: DNAME Redirection in the DNS

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1 Format | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:163-164,545-550` | **Violation**: Dynamic updates can retain empty DNAME RDATA, then lookup treats it as the root target and serves it. §2.1 requires the target field to be present; the CoreDNS fallback comment has no specific recorded justification. | No |
| §2.2 The DNAME Substitution | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:554-555` | **Violation**: An overlong substituted name returns SERVFAIL; §2.2 requires YXDOMAIN. | No |
| §2.2 The DNAME Substitution | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:554-555,727-748` | **Violation**: The early return omits the DNAME and its RRSIG, when signed; §2.2 requires them as proof of YXDOMAIN. | No |
| §2.3 DNAME Owner Name Matching the QNAME | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:664-666,802-810`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:15-47` | **Partial**: Ordinary QTYPEs at the DNAME owner are answered from that owner. The Zone lookup treats ANY as a literal RR type and returns no owner RRset unless the separately configured `any` plugin supplies the RFC 8482 HINFO response. | No |
| §2.3 DNAME Owner Name Matching the QNAME | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:166-188` | **Violation**: Zone construction accepts a non-apex DNAME and NS RR at the same owner. RFC 6672 prohibits that combination outside the zone apex. | No |
| §2.3 DNAME Owner Name Matching the QNAME | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:189-191` | **Partial**: Zone construction requires an SOA, but does not require an apex NS RR when the apex owns a DNAME. | No |
| §2.4 Names next to and below a DNAME Record | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:135-188` | **Violation**: Zone construction permits multiple DNAME RRs at one owner. The dynamic-update builder also lacks a singleton-DNAME check. | No |
| §2.4 Names next to and below a DNAME Record | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:135-188` | **Violation**: Ordinary zone construction permits CNAME and DNAME RRs at the same owner. The dynamic-update builder rejects this conflict, but zone-file loading and `Zone::from_records` do not. | No |
| §2.4 Names next to and below a DNAME Record | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:75-120,135-188` | **Violation**: Zone loading does not refuse a zone that violates the DNAME singleton or coexistence rules, despite RFC 6672 saying a server ought to refuse it. | No |
| §2.4 Names next to and below a DNAME Record | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:75-120` | **Violation**: Zone loading checks no ancestor-zone DNAME before loading a subordinate zone, despite RFC 6672 saying a server ought to refuse that zone. Zones load independently, so this relationship is not checked. | No |
| §3.2 Server Algorithm | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:554-556` | **Violation**: An overlong DNAME substitution returns SERVFAIL before adding the DNAME to the answer. The algorithm requires the DNAME and YXDOMAIN. | No |
| §3.2 Server Algorithm | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:678-683` | **Violation**: The restarted lookup preserves only SERVFAIL and NXDOMAIN; it maps other final-cycle errors, including YXDOMAIN, to NOERROR. RFC 6604 §3 requires the response RCODE to reflect the final query cycle. | No |
| §3.2 Server Algorithm | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:178-213`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:157-161` | **Missing**: Cache lookup requires an exact QNAME, QTYPE, and QCLASS match. It does not search for a cached DNAME at an ancestor when QNAME is absent. | No |
| §3.4 Acceptance and Intermediate Storage | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:139-161`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:189-203` | **Missing**: Cache hits return the stored wire response unchanged. The cache has no DNAME substitution path to synthesize a CNAME on behalf of a client. | No |
| §3.4 Acceptance and Intermediate Storage | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:625-652`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:94-101` | **Violation**: Eligible responses are cached without checking DNAME validation status. This can cache an unvalidated DNAME, despite the RFC's SHOULD NOT. | No |
| §5.2 Dynamic Update and DNAME | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/update.rs:136-151`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:323-324` | **Violation**: When a different DNAME already owns the name, `apply_updates` appends the new RR instead of replacing the existing DNAME. Lookup then selects the first DNAME, so the update does not take effect. | No |
| §5.3.1 Signed DNAME, Unsigned Synthesized CNAME | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:234-259`, `crates/pharos-plugins/tests/functional/dnssec_extra.rs:254-261` | **Violation**: The DNSSEC signer signs every in-zone answer RRset, including the synthesized CNAME. The DNAME test explicitly expects a CNAME signature, contrary to the requirement that synthesized CNAMEs never be signed. | No |

## RFC 6742: DNS Resource Records for the Identifier-Locator Network Protocol (ILNP)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1.2 NID RR Presentation Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:126-133` | **Violation**: The parser checks only one expected separator and ignores other separators and trailing bytes, so `0014:4fffXff20Yee64suffix` is accepted instead of requiring four four-digit hexadecimal groups separated by colons. The comment at line 120 cites §§3.1-3.3, not this requirement. | No |
| §2.2.1 L32 RDATA Wire Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:88-100`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:444-476` | **Partial**: The ordinary L32 parser emits a 16-bit preference followed by four IPv4 octets. The RFC 3597 generic path does not require full consumption for type 105, so it accepts zero- or two-octet RDATA and silently drops trailing octets. | No |
| §2.2.2 L32 RR Presentation Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:142-146` | **Violation**: The zone parser accepts IPv4-mapped IPv6 literals as L32 locators, although the required presentation syntax is four decimal numbers separated by periods. No ADR or plugin README records this extension. | No |
| §2.3.1 The L64 RDATA Wire Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:121-136`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:963-965` | **Partial**: Zone parsing emits a 16-bit preference followed by eight locator octets, but wire parsing treats L64 as opaque and accepts RDATA of any length. The nearby RFC 6742 citation names §§3.1-3.3, not §2.3.1. | No |
| §2.3.1.1 The Preference Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:121-136`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:963-965`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:1260-1296` | **Partial**: Zone-file parsing encodes the preference as two network-order octets. Generic L64 RDATA parsing and building also accept arbitrary-length bytes, so they do not enforce the 16-bit field layout. The nearby RFC 6742 citation names §§3.1-3.3, not §2.3.1.1. | No |
| §2.3.1.2 The Locator64 Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:121-136`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:963-965`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:1260-1296` | **Partial**: Zone-file parsing converts the locator to eight network-order octets. Generic L64 RDATA parsing and building also accept arbitrary bytes, so they do not enforce the 64-bit field layout. The nearby RFC 6742 citation names §§3.1-3.3, not §2.3.1.2. | No |
| §2.3.2 L64 RR Presentation Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:125-136` | **Violation**: The parser accepts non-colon separators and trailing characters in Locator64, though the required presentation has four four-digit hexadecimal groups separated by colons. For example, `0014:4fffXff20Yee64suffix` is accepted; the nearby citation names §§3.1-3.3, not §2.3.2. | No |
| §2.4.1.2 The FQDN Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone.rs:543-550` | **Violation**: Zone parsing does not compare an LP target with its owner, so it accepts an LP record whose target equals its owner. | No |
| §2.4.1.2 The FQDN Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/builder.rs:208-227`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:31-39`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/mod.rs:250-280` | **Partial**: Zone-file parsing and dynamic-update normalization produce uncompressed names, but the public raw-RDATA builder can transmit caller-supplied compressed LP names. | No |

|---|---|---|---|

| §6.2 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "test." — authoritative DNS servers: immediate negative responses | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/file/mod.rs:369-385`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:514-519,684-688` | **Partial**: A configured authoritative zone returns NXDOMAIN for absent names, but names outside configured zones fall through or return REFUSED instead of receiving the required immediate negative response. | No |
| §6.2 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "test." — authoritative DNS servers: special recognition | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/file/mod.rs:369-385` | **Violation**: The file plugin selects only configured zones and has no special-name recognition for `test.` or its subdomains. | No |
| §6.2 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "test." — caching DNS servers: avoid upstream queries by default | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:295-306`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:222-235` | **Violation**: Cache misses continue down the chain, and a matching `forward .` stanza forwards these names upstream. | No |
| §6.2 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "test." — caching DNS servers: immediate negative responses | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:295-306` | **Violation**: Cache misses do not generate immediate negative responses for these names; they delegate to the next plugin. | No |
| §6.2 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "test." — caching DNS servers: special recognition | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:153-169` | **Violation**: Cache eligibility checks query shape and configured zones, but never recognizes `test.` or its subdomains as special. | No |
| §6.3 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "localhost." — 4. Caching DNS Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:55-60, 75-77, 120-133`, `crates/pharos-plugins/tests/allocations/local_allocations.rs:25-52` | **Partial**: The optional `local` plugin answers A/AAAA with loopback and other types negatively for recognized localhost names. At the `localhost.` apex, NS and SOA queries instead receive positive answers, contrary to the required negative response. | Yes |
| §6.3 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "localhost." — 5. Authoritative DNS Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/local/mod.rs:55-60, 75-77, 120-133` | **Partial**: The optional `local` plugin provides the prescribed behavior for most queries, but serves positive NS and SOA answers at the `localhost.` apex instead of negative responses for all non-address types. | Yes |
| §6.3 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "localhost." — 6. DNS Server Operators | — | **Missing**: Operator documentation does not state that the effective RDATA for localhost names is defined by protocol and cannot be modified by local configuration; the `local` RDATA is fixed constants (`local/mod.rs:17-18`), but no operator-facing text records it. | No |
| §6.4 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "invalid." — 4. Caching DNS Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:229-234, crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:153-170` | **Violation**: No special handling blocks `.invalid` queries; a matching forward stanza sends them upstream, and the cache has no `.invalid` exclusion. | No |
| §6.4 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "invalid." — 5. Authoritative DNS Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/file/mod.rs:369-385` | **Violation**: The authoritative file plugin serves configured `.invalid` zones as ordinary zones and has no rule to return immediate negative responses. | No |
| §6.4 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for "invalid." — 6. DNS Server Operators | — | **Missing**: Operator documentation does not state that `.invalid` names are protocol-defined nonexistent and cannot be overridden by local configuration. | No |
| §6.5 Domain Name Reservation Considerations for Example Domains — 6. DNS Server Operators | — | **Missing**: Operator documentation does not state that example names are reserved for documentation. | No |

## RFC 6781: DNSSEC Operational Practices, Version 2

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3.1 Operational Motivation for Zone Signing Keys and Key Signing Keys | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-359` | **Partial**: With both key types configured, signer chooses KSK solely by RR type, so KSK can sign non-apex DNSKEY RRsets; RFC 6781 §3.1 limits KSK signing to the apex DNSKEY RRset. Existing comment cites RFC 4034 §2.1.1, not this operational rule. | No |
| §3.4.1 Signature Algorithm | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:67-75` | **Deviation**: RFC 6781 recommends RSA/SHA-1 as an alternative, but Pharos rejects algorithms 5 and 7. The DNSSEC plugin README documents the rejection because RFC 9905 §2 prohibits these algorithms for signing. | Yes |
| §4.1 Key Rollovers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:52-57,87-118,198-224` | **Partial**: The signer replaces DNSKEY/RRSIG input and regenerates records only from the configured keys. It has no rollover stages or cache-window tracking, so changing keys can remove material needed to validate cached data. | No |
| §4.1.1 Zone Signing Key Rollovers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-359` | **Partial**: The online signer separates KSK and ZSK signatures, but does not stage ZSK rollover or retain old keys for the cache interval; configured keys determine the current DNSKEY and signatures. | No |
| §4.1.1.1 Pre-Publish Zone Signing Key Rollover | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/setup.rs:43-70`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:113-129`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:350-365` | **Partial**: Every configured key has a private signer, appears in the DNSKEY answer, and signs eligible RRsets immediately. The implementation cannot publish a future ZSK without using it to sign data. | No |
| §4.1.2 Key Signing Key Rollovers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:99-129`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:350-365` | **Partial**: Multiple KSKs can be published and can sign the DNSKEY RRset together. The signer does not coordinate parent DS changes or preserve the old KSK through the TTL_DNSKEY and TTL_DS waits required by Verified erratum 4844. | No |
| §4.1.2.1 Special Considerations for RFC 5011 KSK Rollover | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-365` | **Partial**: A revoked KSK remains eligible to sign the DNSKEY RRset, but the implementation does not enforce the additional Maximum Zone TTL publication period before removal. | No |
| §4.1.3 Single-Type Signing Scheme Key Rollover | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-365` | **Partial**: A single configured key can sign both DNSKEY and other RRsets, and multiple keys can overlap. The implementation does not stage the DS change or wait for old DNSKEY/DS data to expire before removing a key. | No |
| §4.1.4 Algorithm Rollovers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:113-129`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:350-409` | **Partial**: Configuring a new algorithm publishes its DNSKEY and signatures together. The signer cannot first publish new-algorithm signatures, wait for old signed data to expire, then publish the DNSKEY and coordinate the parent DS transition. | No |
| §4.1.4.1 Single-Type Signing Scheme Algorithm Rollover | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:113-129`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:350-409` | **Partial**: The signer can use multiple algorithms, but publishes each configured DNSKEY and its signatures together. It cannot perform the required signatures-first cache transition for a single-type key. | No |
| §4.1.4.2 Algorithm Rollover, RFC 5011 Style | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-365` | **Partial**: A revoked key can sign the DNSKEY RRset, but no rollover state ensures the new trust anchor is established before revocation or retains the revoked key for the RFC 5011 period. | No |
| §4.1.4.3 Single Signing Type Algorithm Rollover, RFC 5011 Style | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-365` | **Partial**: Configured ZSKs can sign zone RRsets while KSKs sign DNSKEY, allowing a shadow-key arrangement. The implementation does not enforce a same-algorithm shadow key or stage its introduction and removal around revocation. | No |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Maximum Zone TTL | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:172-183` | **Partial**: The signer sets a 32-day validity period plus up to five days of jitter and preserves source TTLs. It does not ensure the Maximum Zone TTL is shorter than the signature validity period. | No |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Minimum Zone TTL | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:73-85`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:149-155`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:179-183` | **Partial**: The signer accepts zone TTLs and uses them for signed RRsets without checking that the Minimum Zone TTL supports fetching and validating the complete trust chain. | No |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — SOA expiration timing | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:244-265` | **Partial**: SOA refresh, retry, and expire values are used as supplied. The implementation does not relate SOA expiration to the refresh period or signature validity period. | No |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Secondary refresh before signature expiration | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:244-265`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:316-357`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/mod.rs:198-204` | **Partial**: The secondary refreshes on the SOA timer and stops serving only after SOA expiration following failed refreshes. It does not compare refresh or expiry timing with RRSIG expiration, so it can serve expired signatures while the SOA expiry timer remains active. | No |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Signature publication period | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/output.rs:13-46`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/mod.rs:91-100` | **Partial**: The signer checks every five hours and re-signs when a signature has less than seven days remaining. It does not compare this lead time with Maximum Zone TTL, so publication can end less than one TTL before expiration. | No |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Upcoming signature expiration monitoring | `—` | **Missing**: The secondary has no monitor or alert for upcoming RRSIG expiration; its refresh loop checks SOA serials only. | No |

## RFC 6840: Clarifications and Implementation Notes for DNS Security (DNSSEC)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §5.6 Setting the DO Bit on Replies | `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:268-285` | **Violation**: `existing_flags & 0xff00` can retain an upstream DO bit when the query DO bit is clear, so the response does not copy the query bit. No recorded justification covers this difference. | No |
| §5.11 Mandatory Algorithm Rules | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-359,365-412` | **Violation**: With KSK and ZSK keys using different algorithms, DNSKEY RRsets are signed only with KSK algorithms and other RRsets only with ZSK algorithms. Setup permits this configuration, but RFC 6840 requires every RRset to be signed by at least one key of each algorithm in the DNSKEY RRset. | No |

## RFC 6891: Extension Mechanisms for DNS (EDNS(0))

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §6.1.1 Basic Elements | `crates/pharos-proto/src/message.rs:203-247`, `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:45-47,68-75`, `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:219-223` | **Partial**: Valid EDNS requests receive an OPT in the response. Malformed or duplicate-OPT requests get a header-only FORMERR, despite containing an OPT. | No |
| §6.1.1 Basic Elements | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone.rs:447-550`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:88-100,409-455` | **Violation**: The master-file parser accepts generic TYPE41 RRs and returns them as zone records. OPT can therefore be loaded from and stored in zone data. | No |
| §6.1.1 Basic Elements | `crates/pharos-proto/src/message.rs:224-235`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/tsig.rs:586-638`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:253-265` | **Partial**: TSIG parsing enforces final position, but `Message::parse` accepts SIG(0) before OPT and `forward` relays that wire message unchanged. | No |
| §6.1.1 Basic Elements | `crates/pharos-proto/src/builder.rs:210-269,275-292,386-404` | **Violation**: `record` accepts OPT without tracking it, then either finalizer can append another OPT. The builder can therefore emit a message with duplicate OPT records. | No |
| §6.1.4 Flags | `crates/pharos-proto/src/edns.rs:152-156`, `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:268-285` | **Violation**: Response construction preserves reserved flag bits from the request or existing response OPT. A sender can therefore emit nonzero Z bits instead of setting them to zero. | No |
| §6.1.4 Flags | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/nsid/mod.rs:114-117`, `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:282-285` | **Violation**: NSID synthesizes DO=1, and final response scrubbing preserves it when the request cleared DO. RFC 3225 §3 requires the response DO bit to copy the query. | No |
| §7 Transport Considerations | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:45-47,68-75`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/message.rs:224-232` | **Violation**: A malformed EDNS option makes `Message::parse` fail; admission returns a header-only FORMERR with no OPT. RFC 6891 requires an OPT in this error response to distinguish EDNS format errors from servers without EDNS support. | No |
| §7 Transport Considerations | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:34-36,68-75` | **Violation**: A valid EDNS request with an unsupported opcode receives a header-only NOTIMP response. This omits both the Question and OPT required in a minimal EDNS response. | No |

## RFC 6895: Domain Name System (DNS) IANA Considerations

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2 DNS Query/Response Headers | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:68-74,77-86` | **Partial**: `write_error` copies OpCode, RD, and CD, but `header_error` copies TC, RA, AD, RD, and CD from the Query via mask `0x03b0`, so error Responses carry Query values for bits §2 says only RD and CD are expected to be copied. No RFC 6895 citation, ADR, or plugin README documents this difference. | No |
| §2.3 RCODE Assignment | `crates/pharos-proto/src/codes.rs:62,73-75` | **Partial**: RCODE 16 has distinct BADVERS and BADSIG meanings in OPT and TSIG contexts, but context-free `Display` always returns BADSIG. | No |
| §2.3 RCODE Assignment | `crates/pharos-server/src/metrics.rs:1846-1861` | **Partial**: Metrics always label RCODE 16 as BADSIG, including OPT-extended BADVERS responses. Also `metrics.rs:40` cites RFC 6895 §2.3 for "assigned RCODEs fit in 0 through 23", but §2.3 lists 23 as unassigned (BADCOOKIE is RFC 7873); miscitation. | No |
| §3 DNS Resource Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:618-627` | **Deviation**: The cache raises a record TTL of zero to its configured minimum before checking eligibility; the default minimum is 5 seconds, so it caches and serves data beyond the transaction. The cache README documents this CoreDNS-compatible behavior. | Yes |
| §3 DNS Resource Records | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone.rs:870-895` | **Violation**: Wrapping arithmetic can turn an over-range decimal TTL into a value at or below `u32::MAX`, so the parser accepts a value outside the 32-bit unsigned TTL range. | No |
| §3.1 RRTYPE IANA Considerations | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:42-50` | **Violation**: `type_bitmap` permits RRTYPE 128 as zone data, but 128–255 are allocated only for QTYPEs and Meta-TYPEs. | No |
| §3.1 RRTYPE IANA Considerations | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone.rs:453-462,912-921` | **Violation**: The zone parser rejects ANY but accepts other query-only and meta types, including AXFR, OPT, and TYPE0, as zone RR types. | No |
| §3.2 RR CLASS IANA Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:70-71,277-286,613-666` | **Partial**: Zone records are indexed by owner and type, not CLASS, and lookup does not check the query QCLASS. A zone with the same owner and type in multiple classes can return records from the wrong class when non-IN queries are admitted. | No |
| §3.2 RR CLASS IANA Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/whoami/mod.rs:25-30,71-95` | **Violation**: With no question, the code defaults to reserved CLASS 0 and emits A/AAAA and SRV records in that class. | No |
| §3.2 RR CLASS IANA Considerations | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone.rs:898-909` | **Violation**: The zone parser accepts QCLASS NONE as an ordinary zone RR class via `parse_class`, though it is meaningful only in queries or updates. ANY is rejected earlier at line 453, and CLASS0 is accepted as reserved. | No |
| §3.3.2 Label Contents and Use | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:70-71,277-286,613-666` | **Partial**: Names are class-local, but zone lookup ignores CLASS and can mix records from different classes; see §3.2. | No |

## RFC 7477: Child-to-Parent Synchronization in DNS

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1.1.2 Flags Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:139`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:440` | **Violation**: The typed CSYNC encoder (advanced.rs:139) and the generic `62 => b"42b"` layout (rdata.rs:440) copy the 16-bit flags unchecked, so nonzero undefined flag bits pass; RFC 7477 §2.1.1.2 requires publishers to set undefined flags to zero. | No |

No ADR or plugin README justifies it. Existing citations name §4 and §4.5, not §4.4. | No |

## RFC 7766: DNS Transport over TCP - Implementation Requirements

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §4 Discussion — TC retry | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:407-424`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/README.md:9` | **Deviation**: The forwarder retries a truncated UDP response over TCP only when `prefer_udp` is set; otherwise it returns the truncated response. The README records this CoreDNS-compatible behavior. | Yes |
| §5 Transport Protocol Selection | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/resolve.rs:56-84` | **Partial**: Configured resolver lookups use UDP only and do not retry truncated responses over TCP; hostname resolution through the system resolver is separate. | No |
| §5 Transport Protocol Selection | `crates/pharos-server/src/lib.rs:826-829, 902-904` | **Violation**: `Handler::udp_workers` and `Server.udp_workers` accept zero, and the listener then binds no UDP sockets while still binding TCP, so that configuration does not support both required transports. The Corefile `multisocket` path rejects zero and `worker_count` floors at 1, so only the Rust API reaches it. | Yes |
| §6.2.1 Connection Reuse | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:182-200`, `crates/pharos-server/src/transfer.rs:165` | **Violation**: The secondary opens a new TCP connection for each SOA check and AXFR. RFC 9103 §6.3.1 also recommends that XFR clients reuse an open connection; no justification is recorded. | No |
| §6.2.1.1 Query Pipelining | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:446-462, 491-521, 572-585` | **Violation**: The forwarding client leases one socket for one query and returns it to the idle pool only after its response arrives. It therefore does not pipeline regular queries. | No |
| §6.2.1.1 Query Pipelining | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:446-462, 491-521, 572-585` | **Violation**: The forwarding client waits for each outstanding response before it can send another query on that connection, contrary to the explicit SHOULD NOT. RFC 9103's exception applies only when all queries are XFRs. | No |
| §6.2.2 Concurrent Connections | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:433-475, 572-585` | **Violation**: Concurrent cache misses each dial a new upstream TCP connection. `max_idle_conns` limits only idle sockets, so active connection count is unbounded and does not minimize per-server connections. | No |
| §6.2.2 Concurrent Connections | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:433-475, 572-585` | **Violation**: The client does not follow the recommendation to keep at most one regular-query connection per protocol and interaction. RFC 9103 §6.4 updates the connection-purpose guidance but does not permit unbounded regular-query connections. | No |
| §6.2.3 Idle Timeouts | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:243-272, 396-406`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/README.md:10` | **Deviation**: The client only sweeps expired idle sockets when another exchange starts. With no later traffic, cached TCP sessions remain open indefinitely; the README documents this behavior. | Yes |
| §6.2.3 Idle Timeouts | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:243-272, 572-585`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/README.md:10` | **Deviation**: The client keeps idle TCP sessions without an idle-timeout signaling mechanism, and the configured expiry is not enforced without another exchange; the README documents this behavior. | Yes |
| §6.2.4 Teardown | `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:186-189, 280-286, 310` | **Violation**: When frame reading ends, the server drains in-flight query tasks. Those tasks still try to write responses, even when the peer closed the session before all responses were sent. | No |
| §8 TCP Message Length Field | `crates/pharos-server/src/transfer.rs:165-173`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:181-199` | **Partial**: The production transfer clients write the length and Message separately; the shared server helper `write_tcp_frame` uses a vectored write. | No |

## RFC 7828: The edns-tcp-keepalive EDNS0 Option

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3.1 Option Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:622-623`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_strictness.rs:131-136` | **Violation**: Generic OPT RDATA parsing rewrites a two-octet TIMEOUT 0 as a zero-length option, erasing its distinct close-soon meaning. | No |
| §3.2.1 Sending Queries | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:297-301`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:407-414, 488-491` | **Violation**: The forward path selects UDP for UDP Queries by default and preserves option bytes, so option 11 can be sent upstream over UDP despite the MUST NOT. | No |
| §3.2.1 Sending Queries | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:488-491` | **Violation**: The proxy copies a downstream Query's two-octet TIMEOUT unchanged into TCP Queries, though clients MUST send zero-length option data. The parser's two-octet acceptance is required for Responses and is not the violation. | No |
| §3.2.2 Receiving Responses | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:501-514, 551-585` | **Partial**: The proxy never reads or honors a TCP response timeout; cached connections instead use local `expire`, which can exceed the server's advertised timeout. | No |
| §3.2.2 Receiving Responses | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:551-585` | **Violation**: The proxy ignores TIMEOUT 0 and caches the TCP socket, so it may send later Queries despite the recommendation to stop and close. | No |
| §3.4 TCP Session Management | `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:20-21, 41` | **Partial**: The listener caps accepted TCP connections at 1,024, but does not establish the limit through configuration or process-limit interrogation. | No |
| §3.5 Non-clean Paths | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:488-491, 501-523` | **Missing**: The proxy forwards option 11 but has no fallback for an intermediary that blocks or disrupts EDNS options. | No |
| §4 Intermediary Considerations | `crates/pharos-proto/src/edns_options.rs:257-262`, `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:219-223`, `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:146-170` | **Partial**: Pharos can parse, copy, or generically emit option 11, but does not integrate it with TCP session timeout management. | No |

## RFC 7871: Client Subnet in DNS Queries

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §5 Overview — ECS only for IN class | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:1469-1495` | **Violation**: The subnet rewrite inserts ECS without checking the question class, so it can add ECS to non-IN queries. | No |
| §6 Option Format | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:253-266` | **Violation**: `forward` relays a client ECS option unchanged, so a nonzero SCOPE PREFIX-LENGTH reaches an upstream query, though §6 requires SCOPE 0 in queries. | No |
| §7.1.1 Recursive Resolvers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:1233-1251, 1486-1490` | **Violation**: `rewrite edns0 subnet` sets SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH from the configured value, not the shorter of the incoming SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH and the server's maximum cacheable prefix. | No |
| §7.1.1 Recursive Resolvers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:261-276, 1486-1490` | **Partial**: The configured prefix is operator-chosen and may be /32 or /128; the SHOULD that SOURCE be shorter than the full address is not enforced. | No |
| §7.1.1 Recursive Resolvers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:1486-1490` | **Missing**: When an incoming ECS option is not forwarded, the SHOULD to return REFUSED is not implemented; the `set` and `replace` actions overwrite the incoming option and answer normally. | No |
| §7.1.2 Stub Resolvers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:1233-1251, 1486-1490, 2122-2131` | **Violation**: `rewrite edns0 subnet` builds ECS from the transport client address using configured prefix lengths, without capping them to an incoming ECS SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH. An incoming `/16` can therefore become an outgoing `/24`, exposing more client-address bits than permitted. | No |
| §7.1.2 Stub Resolvers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:1233-1251, 1486-1490, 2122-2131` | **Violation**: With an incoming SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH of 0, `rewrite edns0 subnet` still adds client address bits instead of omitting the client address from its upstream query. | No |
| §7.1.3 Forwarding Resolvers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:1486-1490` | **Partial**: Transparent forwarding preserves the client's ECS prefix, but `rewrite edns0 subnet` replaces it with configured IPv4/IPv6 prefixes from the peer address. A rule can therefore send more client-address bits than the incoming SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH permits. | No |
| §7.1.3 Forwarding Resolvers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:334-382` | **Violation**: On REFUSED, `forward` does not retry without ADDRESS. Configured failover retries the unchanged query, so a non-zero ECS address remains present. | No |
| §7.2.1 Authoritative Nameserver | `crates/pharos-proto/src/edns_options.rs:270-293` | **Partial**: The parser rejects unsupported families, but accepts FAMILY 0 with SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH 0 and treats its address as IPv4. This undefined family is malformed, yet admission will not return FORMERR. | No |
| §7.2.2 Intermediate Nameserver | `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:275-287` | **Violation**: When the client sent an OPT without ECS, the Listener preserves all options from an upstream Response OPT. An upstream ECS option can therefore reach a client that did not send ECS. | No |
| §7.2.2 Intermediate Nameserver | `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:288-295`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/edns.rs:197-199` | **Violation**: If the client sent ECS but the upstream Response has no OPT, the Listener builds an OPT from registered Query options only. ECS is not registered by default, so the required ECS response option is omitted. | No |
| §7.3 Handling ECS Responses and Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:625-652`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Partial**: The cache stores eligible, non-truncated responses, but its key omits ECS family, source prefix, address, and scope. It therefore does not cache by the ECS option data. | No |
| §7.3 Handling ECS Responses and Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:415-425` | **Partial**: UDP responses with TC trigger a TCP retry only when `prefer_udp` is enabled; the default UDP forwarding path does not retry. | No |
| §7.3 Handling ECS Responses and Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:560-568` | **Violation**: The proxy checks the response question tuple but does not compare ECS FAMILY, SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH, or the corresponding ADDRESS bits before accepting the response. | No |
| §7.3 Handling ECS Responses and Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:380-397` | **Missing**: A REFUSED response may trigger configured upstream failover, but Pharos does not retry the query without ECS. | No |
| §7.3.1 Caching the Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:301-344` | **Violation**: Answer records from an ECS response are stored under a key that omits ECS and can be served to clients on other networks. | No |
| §7.3.1 Caching the Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Missing**: The cache does not store SCOPE PREFIX-LENGTH bits or mark the resulting network range as valid when scope is no longer than source prefix. | No |
| §7.3.1 Caching the Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Missing**: The cache has no configured maximum ECS prefix length and does not use SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH bits as the cache scope when it reaches that maximum. | No |
| §7.3.1 Caching the Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Missing**: The cache does not create exact-SOURCE-PREFIX-LENGTH entries when source is below the configured maximum and scope exceeds source. | No |
| §7.3.1 Caching the Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Missing**: The cache has no ECS-aware distinction between `/0` entries caused by a query source prefix of zero and entries caused by a response scope prefix of zero. | No |
| §7.3.1 Caching the Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Partial**: Pharos caches DNS responses, but does not implement network-specific ECS caching or its prefix-aware reuse rules. | No |
| §7.3.1 Caching the Response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:49-55`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Partial**: The cache has global entry and TTL limits, but no ECS-specific limits on networks or answers per query, nor a prefix limit tied to what it will cache. | No |
| §7.3.2 Answering from Cache | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:178-212`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Partial**: Lookup begins with the question tuple, but the cache does not select an ECS RRset by longest-prefix match. | No |
| §7.3.2 Answering from Cache | — | **Missing**: For a query without ECS, the cache does not use the client's address to choose among network-scoped entries. | No |
| §7.3.2 Answering from Cache | — | **Missing**: For ECS queries, the cache does not use the client address with SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH initially ignored to find a covering entry. | No |
| §7.3.2 Answering from Cache | — | **Missing**: When no covering entry exists and the query source prefix is below the cache maximum, the cache does not retry lookup for an exact source-prefix entry. | No |
| §7.3.2 Answering from Cache | — | **Missing**: The cache cannot find a matching network-scoped entry and generate a response from its unexpired data as specified in Section 7.2. | No |
| §7.3.2 Answering from Cache | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:178-212` | **Violation**: A question-key hit is served without checking whether its ECS network matches; Pharos can return a tailored response instead of resolving when no matching network entry exists. | No |
| §7.5 Transitivity | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:253-266`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:488-492`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:651-668`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-780`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:285-344` | **Violation**: `forward` relays client ECS options, with only the DNS transaction ID changed, but `cache` keys only on name, type, class, DO, and CD, then omits the response OPT when storing. It cannot separate ECS prefixes or honor response scope; no ADR or plugin README documents this gap. | No |
| §11.1 Privacy — concealing client address | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:261-276,1486-1490` | **Partial**: The subnet rewrite truncates the client address to an operator-selected prefix, but permits full-width /32 and /128 prefixes and does not enforce the recommended privacy lengths. | No |
| §11.1 Privacy — preserving an incoming privacy limit | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:1417-1429,1486-1490` | **Violation**: A configured subnet rewrite replaces an existing ECS option with one derived from the transport client address, without honoring an incoming SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH such as /0. This can disclose address bits the client excluded. | No |
| §11.2 Birthday Attacks — discard mismatched response | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:551-567` | **Violation**: A response with mismatched ECS fields passes validation and is returned; the proxy does not discard it. | No |
| §11.2 Birthday Attacks — verify ECS response fields | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:551-567` | **Violation**: The forward proxy checks response ID, QR, and questions, but does not compare ECS FAMILY, ADDRESS, or SOURCE PREFIX-LENGTH with the query before accepting the response. | No |
| §11.3 Cache Pollution — do not send beyond cacheable prefix | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/rewrite/mod.rs:261-276,1486-1490; crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-780` | **Violation**: The subnet rewrite can send any configured prefix up to /32 or /128, while the cache has no ECS-aware key or prefix limit. A cache-and-forward chain cannot constrain sent address bits to a network-specific cache scope. | No |
| §11.3 Cache Pollution — scope cached ECS answers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-780; crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:285-328` | **Partial**: Cache capacity is bounded, but the key omits ECS and cached response storage removes the OPT record. An ECS-tailored answer can therefore populate the generic name/type/class entry and be reused across networks. | No |

## RFC 8020: NXDOMAIN: There Really Is Nothing Underneath

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §1 Introduction and Background: NXDOMAIN subtree meaning | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:804-808`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/README.md:27` | **Deviation**: Kubernetes plugin returns NXDOMAIN for names with no records of their own even when descendants exist. Its README records this as CoreDNS parity. | Yes |
| §2 Rules: NXDOMAIN cut | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:157-161`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779` | **Partial**: The cache stores NXDOMAIN denials (`cache/mod.rs:582-585`) and answers the exact denied question from them, but names and RRsets below the denied name are not treated as unreachable and are still forwarded. §6 lets non-tree caches skip the cut, but no ADR or cache README records that choice. | No |
| §3.1 Updates to RFC 1034 | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:541-544,563-581,684-687` | **Partial**: Empty non-terminals usually produce NOERROR with no answer. Wildcard synthesis checks `!exact` rather than whether the QNAME exists, so an ancestor wildcard can answer an existing empty non-terminal instead of returning NODATA. | No |
| §3.2 Updates to RFC 2308: descendant reuse | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:767-779`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:157-161` | **Missing**: The revised rule says a cached NXDOMAIN should also answer queries for descendants of the original QNAME in the same class. The cache key is the exact question (`cache/mod.rs:767-779`) and `serve` requires an exact name match (`cache/store.rs:157-161`); no descendant lookup exists. ADR 0002 defaults to RFC behaviour and the cache README records no justification. | No |
| §5 Possible Issues: ENT answered with NXDOMAIN | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:804-808`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/README.md:27` | **Deviation**: §5 calls an authoritative NXDOMAIN for an empty non-terminal "definitely wrong". Kubernetes returns NXDOMAIN for names with no records of their own (for example `svc.<zone>`) even when descendants exist. The README records this as CoreDNS parity. | Yes |
