## RFC 8482: Providing Minimal-Sized Responses to DNS Queries That Have QTYPE=ANY

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §4 Behavior of DNS Responders | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:11-47`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/README.md:7` | **Deviation**: The plugin synthesizes HINFO before checking whether the QNAME exists, instead of following standard lookup behavior for nonexistent names. The README documents synthesis before backend lookup. | Yes |
| §4 Behavior of DNS Responders | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:35-45`, `crates/pharos-plugins/tests/functional/any.rs:39-46` | **Violation**: The plugin answers a QCLASS=CH query with an IN-class HINFO record. This ANY-response mode is specified for QCLASS=IN, and no documentation justifies the CH behavior. | No |
| §4.2 Synthesized HINFO RRset — CNAME owner | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:15-47`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/README.md:7` | **Deviation**: `any` synthesizes HINFO before checking whether the owner has a CNAME. The README records this CoreDNS-compatible behavior. | Yes |
| §4.2 Synthesized HINFO RRset — CPU field | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:37-47` | **Deviation**: RFC recommends CPU value `RFC8482`; Pharos sends `ANY obsoleted`. The plugin documents CoreDNS-compatible HINFO synthesis. | Yes |
| §4.2 Synthesized HINFO RRset — OS field | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:37-47` | **Deviation**: RFC recommends an empty OS string; Pharos sends `See RFC 8482`. The plugin documents CoreDNS-compatible HINFO synthesis. | Yes |
| §4.2 Synthesized HINFO RRset — TTL policy | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:37-47` | **Partial**: The fixed 8482-second TTL cannot be chosen by the operator to suppress repeat queries while accounting for local policy changes. | No |
| §4.2 Synthesized HINFO RRset — configurable TTL | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:37-47` | **Violation**: The synthesized TTL is hard-coded, not configurable by the operator as recommended. | No |
| §4.2 Synthesized HINFO RRset — signed Zone with DO=1 | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:37-47`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/README.md:7` | **Deviation**: The plugin returns no RRSIG for its synthesized answer when DO=1, including for signed Zones. The README records this CoreDNS-compatible limitation. | Yes |
| §6 HINFO Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/mod.rs:37-47`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/any/setup.rs:7-11` | **Partial**: The opt-in `any` plugin always synthesizes HINFO and has no setting to choose a single RRset or another type. Its README records unconditional HINFO synthesis, but not §6's recommendation for zones that rely on HINFO; operators can avoid this mode only by omitting the plugin. | No |

## RFC 8490: DNS Stateful Operations

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §4.2 Applicable Transports | `xtask/src/query.rs:93-97,221-230` | **Violation**: `+opcode=stateful` accepts UDP, although RFC 8490 currently defines DSO only over TCP or TLS. | No |
| §5.4 Message Format | `xtask/src/query.rs:316-379` | **Violation**: The DSO query builder always emits a Question and may add OPT or TSIG records, despite DSO requiring all four section counts to be zero. | No |
| §5.4 Message Format | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:34-37` | **Violation**: DSO messages with nonzero section counts reach the unsupported-opcode branch and receive NOTIMP. RFC 8490 §5.4 requires FORMERR for any nonzero count. | No |
| §5.4.1 DNS Header Fields in DSO Messages | `xtask/src/query.rs:319-320` | **Violation**: With `+opcode=stateful`, the builder sets every message ID to zero, but a DSO request requires a unique nonzero ID. | No |
| §5.4.1 DNS Header Fields in DSO Messages | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:34-36,68-74` | **Violation**: a DSO unidirectional message (QR=0, MESSAGE ID zero) receives a QR=1 NOTIMP response that copies ID zero. RFC 8490 §5.4.1 requires DSO response IDs to be nonzero. | No |
| §5.4.1 DNS Header Fields in DSO Messages | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:70` | **Violation**: the response flag mask preserves AD/CD bits from the request. These bits are reserved Z bits in DSO messages and must be zero in transmitted DSO messages. | No |
| §5.4.3 DSO Unidirectional Messages | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:34-36,68-74` | **Violation**: a DSO message with QR=0 and MESSAGE ID zero receives NOTIMP. RFC 8490 §5.4.3 requires no response to such a message. | No |
| §5.5 Message Handling | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:33-36,68-74` | **Violation**: A DSO message with QR=0 and MESSAGE ID zero receives NOTIMP. RFC 8490 requires unidirectional messages to elicit no response. | No |
| §5.5.3 Error Responses | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:34-36,68-75`, `crates/pharos-server/src/server.rs:426-447`, `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:207-213,280-293` | **Violation**: an unsupported DSO unidirectional message (MESSAGE ID zero) receives a NOTIMP response, and TCP keeps the connection open. RFC 8490 §5.5.3 says an unexpected DSO unidirectional message SHOULD cause the receiver to forcibly abort the connection. | No |

## RFC 8767: Serving Stale Data to Improve DNS Resiliency

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §4 Standards Action — stale response TTL | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:144-148`; `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:179-185` | **Violation**: `stale_ttl` defaults to zero; with the default `keepttl` setting, stale responses therefore carry TTL 0, despite the MUST to return stale records with TTL greater than 0 and the RECOMMENDED 30-second value. | No |
| §4 Standards Action — zero-TTL cache | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:591-618,648-652` | **Violation**: The cache raises zero-TTL records to its configured minimum and stores them. The README records this behavior but gives no standards rationale. | Yes |
| §7 Implementation Caveats | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:218-245, crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:285-290` | **Deviation**: Default immediate mode serves stale before refresh failure is known. ADR 0002 permits CoreDNS-compatible behavior. | Yes |

The cache README records the raise only in its differential scenario (`crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/README.md:28-30`) with no justification, matching the §4 zero-TTL row. | Yes |

## RFC 8777: DNS Reverse IP Automatic Multicast Tunneling (AMT) Discovery

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §4.2 RData Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:31,963`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:1260-1295` | **Partial**: Zone text can encode AMTRELAY, but wire parsing rejects it (AMTRELAY is in `unsupported_name_type`, rdata.rs:31, so rdata.rs:963 returns an error) and the `Unknown` writer rejects it too (rdata.rs:1260-1295). Valid records therefore cannot pass through typed parse/rebuild. | No |
| §4.2 RData Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:163-165` | **Violation**: The zone encoder accepts type values 128–255 and ORs them into the D/type octet, although type is only 7 bits. Values with the high bit set can alter D. Existing RFC 8777 §4 citation covers address encoding, not this bound. | No |
| §4.2.3 RData Format - Type | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:163-176` | **Violation**: With D=0, types 0–3 use their defined relay encodings (type 3 uncompressed). With D=1, the `out[1] < 128` check at advanced.rs:174 drops the relay bytes for every type, so types 1–3 lose the address or name their type defines. No ADR, plugin README, or `docs/compatibility.md` entry justifies this; zone_advanced.rs:257 asserts the truncated `[10, 0x83]` for type 3. | No |
| §4.2.4 RData Format - Relay | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:167-176` | **Violation**: With D=0, the empty, IPv4, IPv6, and uncompressed-name relay fields are encoded, including the root label that Erratum 6218 requires. With D=1, advanced.rs:174 omits the relay field for types 1–3, though the field is required for each type. No ADR, plugin README, or `docs/compatibility.md` entry justifies this. | No |
| §4.3.1 Representation of AMTRELAY RRs | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:146-171` | **Violation**: The parser requires the presentation fields and enforces `.` for type 0, but accepts an 8-bit type value above 127. It then treats the high bit as D, silently changing the supplied type; the relay type is only 7 bits. | No |
| §5 IANA Considerations | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:163-165` | **Violation**: Verified Erratum 6688 makes Relay Type a 7-bit field, but parsing accepts 128–255 and ORs those bits into D; input 128 is silently encoded as D=1, Relay Type=0. The nearby citation names §4, not §5. | No |

## RFC 8914: Extended DNS Errors

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2 Extended DNS Error EDNS0 Option Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/edns_options.rs:196-209` | **Partial**: RFC defines EXTRA-TEXT as UTF-8, but the public encoder accepts arbitrary bytes and emits them unchanged. Template text is a Rust `String`, but other callers can encode invalid UTF-8; the template encoder comment also cites §4 instead of §2. | No |
| §3 Extended DNS Error Processing | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/setup.rs:118-124`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/mod.rs:740-747` | **Partial**: `ederror` accepts text up to the wire limit (65,533 bytes) without limiting or warning on lengthy EXTRA-TEXT, which can consume the response budget and trigger truncation. | No |
| §4.1 Extended DNS Error Code 0 - Other | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/setup.rs:118-124`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/mod.rs:740-748` | **Partial**: `ederror` accepts code 0 without a reason and serializes empty EXTRA-TEXT, though RFC 8914 says implementations SHOULD add explanatory text. A reason can be configured, but the nearby citation names §4 rather than §4.1. | No |
| §4.16 Extended DNS Error Code 15 - Blocked | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/acl/mod.rs:251-288`, `crates/pharos-plugins/tests/functional/acl.rs:127-165` | **Violation**: ACL emits EDE 15 for client-network or query-type policy blocks, while §4.16 defines it for domains blocked by the operator's internal security policy; the code cites only parent §4. | No |
| §4.18 Extended DNS Error Code 17 - Filtered | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/acl/mod.rs:251-288`, `crates/pharos-plugins/tests/functional/acl.rs:95-109,392-413` | **Violation**: The server-configured `filter` action emits EDE 17, which §4.18 reserves for filtering requested by the client; the code cites only parent §4. | No |
| §4.21 Extended DNS Error Code 20 - Not Authoritative | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/auto/mod.rs:191-197`, `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:77-85`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/setup.rs:118-124`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/mod.rs:740-760` | **Partial**: The auto plugin can return REFUSED for a missing authoritative zone, but the shared response path emits no EDE 20. Template rules can configure EDE 20 manually; no automatic handling covers the specified authoritative-server conditions. | No |
| §4.21 Extended DNS Error Code 20 - Not Authoritative | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:236-245`, `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:77-85`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/setup.rs:118-124`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/template/mod.rs:740-760` | **Partial**: The forward plugin returns REFUSED when its concurrency limit is reached, and that response has no EDE 20. No code checks RD, so a REFUSED answer to a query with RD clear also carries no EDE 20. Template rules can configure EDE 20 manually. | No |

## RFC 8945: Secret Key Transaction Authentication for DNS (TSIG)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §5.2 Server Processing of Request | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:58-64, 81-95`; `crates/pharos-proto/src/tsig.rs:588-595` | **Partial**: The parser rejects TSIG in Answer or Authority, and the plugin rejects duplicate or non-final Additional records for every Query it serves, before zone matching. A TSIG in Answer or Authority of a Query outside a configured zone is ignored instead of being dropped with FORMERR. | No |
| §5.2 Server Processing of Request | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:65-95, 123-178`; `crates/pharos-proto/src/message.rs:494-503` | **Partial**: A correctly placed TSIG is retained, removed, and excluded from ARCOUNT when the request has a question matching a configured zone. Requests outside that path do not get TSIG processing. | No |
| §5.2 Server Processing of Request | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:123-126` | **Partial**: An uninterpretable TSIG maps to FORMERR once the plugin recognizes it, but TSIG outside Additional or requests outside a configured zone bypass this check. Erratum 7983 correctly changes the destination to the client. | No |
| §5.2 Server Processing of Request | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:65-95, 166-178`; `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:145-148`; `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:34-36`; `crates/pharos-server/src/server.rs:499-501`; `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/README.md:8,11` | **Partial**: A valid TSIG request gets a signed response only after the plugin matches a configured zone and question and UPDATE admission passes. A valid TSIG outside that path, including a rejected UPDATE, can receive an unsigned response. | Yes |
| §5.2.1 Key Check and Error Handling | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:151-155`; `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/README.md:11` | **Deviation**: A known key with an unsupported algorithm returns NOTAUTH/BADSIG instead of required NOTAUTH/BADKEY. Plugin README records this CoreDNS-compatibility choice. | Yes |
| §5.2.1 Key Check and Error Handling | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:138-164` | **Missing**: Unknown-key and unsupported-algorithm errors return responses but are not logged, despite the SHOULD. | No |
| §5.2.2 MAC Check and Error Handling | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:160-165`; `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:341-370` | **Missing**: MAC failure is checked and returns unsigned NOTAUTH/BADSIG, but code does not log the error, despite the SHOULD. | No |
| §5.2.3 Time Check and Error Handling | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:157-165` | **Deviation**: the server does not cache the latest received Time Signed value. The TSIG README explicitly says replay detection is not implemented. | Yes |
| §5.2.3 Time Check and Error Handling | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:157-165` | **Deviation**: the server does not reject a request whose Time Signed value predates an earlier request. The TSIG README explicitly says replay detection is not implemented. | Yes |
| §5.2.3 Time Check and Error Handling | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:162-164` | **Missing**: BADTIME responses return without logging the error, despite the SHOULD. | No |
| §5.3 Generation of TSIG on Answers | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:57-64`, `crates/pharos-server/src/server.rs:497-535`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/README.md:11` | **Deviation**: admission-level errors, including BADVERS and rejected UPDATE, bypass TSIG processing and remain unsigned for signed requests. The TSIG README records this CoreDNS-compatible behavior. | Yes |
| §5.3.1 TSIG on TCP Connections | `crates/pharos-proto/src/tsig.rs:418-437` | **Partial**: the normal stream signer signs Responses, but the public `TsigStream::add_unsigned` lets a caller emit unsigned intermediary Responses. Its doc comment states RFC 8945 requires senders to sign every Response and limits the method to legacy interoperability, so the requirement is only partly met by the API. | No |
| §5.4 Client Processing of Answer | `xtask/src/query.rs:255-290` | **Violation**: The TSIG response checker accepts unsigned BADKEY/BADSIG errors without requiring DNS RCODE NOTAUTH; RFC 8945 requires discarding such unsigned errors under any other RCODE. | No |
| §5.5 Special Considerations for Forwarding Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:138-140` | **Violation**: When the TSIG plugin covers the Query's zone, an unknown key returns BADKEY instead of forwarding the TSIG Message unchanged. | No |
| §5.5 Special Considerations for Forwarding Servers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:177-178`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:395-397`, `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:145-148` | **Violation**: A verified TSIG is removed before forwarding, and the unsigned upstream Response is signed without clearing AD. Section 5.5 requires clearing AD when the forwarded Query has no transaction security and the Response has AD set. | No |
| §6 Algorithms and Identifiers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/setup.rs:99-107`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:144-160` | **Partial**: SHA-1 remains supported as required for compatibility, but setup enables it for every key and offers no policy to replace existing SHA-1 use with SHA-2 as recommended. | No |
| §7 TSIG Truncation Policy | `crates/pharos-proto/src/tsig.rs:127-150` | **Partial**: The default signer emits a full-length Response MAC, but `sign_truncated` permits one shorter than the validated Query MAC. RFC 8945 says a reply SHOULD be at least as long as its Query MAC. | No |
| §8 Shared Secrets | `crates/pharos-proto/src/tsig_key.rs:33-45`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/tsig.rs:68-79`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/setup.rs:99-108` | **Violation**: Any decodable secret length is accepted and passed to HMAC. This permits secrets shorter than the keyed hash output, contrary to §8's SHOULD. | No |
| §10 Security Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/setup.rs:99-107`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:144-155` | **Deviation**: one configured key name and secret create keys for all five supported algorithms, contrary to the recommendation to associate only one algorithm with a key name. ADR 0002 permits CoreDNS-compatible behavior, and `crates/pharos/compatibility.md:243` records this behavior; neither gives a section-specific citation. | No |

## RFC 8976: Message Digest for DNS Zones

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.2.3 Hash Algorithm Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:927-935,1215-1227`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:274-285` | **Violation**: Wire parsing and encoding accept any digest length of at least 12 octets for hash algorithm 1. RFC 8976 requires SHA384 digests to be exactly 48 octets. | No |
| §2.2.3 Hash Algorithm Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:927-935,1215-1227`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:274-285` | **Violation**: Wire parsing and encoding accept any digest length of at least 12 octets for hash algorithm 2. RFC 8976 requires SHA512 digests to be exactly 64 octets. | No |
| §2.2.4 Digest Field | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:927-935,1215-1227`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:274-285`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_strictness.rs:32-42` | **Partial**: Wire parsing and encoding enforce the 12-octet minimum, but master-file parsing accepts empty or shorter digests; the strictness test asserts both cases. | Yes |
| §2.5 Including ZONEMD RRs in a Zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:194-223` | **Violation**: Zone construction retains each ZONEMD record without checking for duplicate `(Scheme, Hash Algorithm)` tuples at an owner. A zone with distinct digests but a repeated tuple can load and be served. | No |

When SOA TTL is lower, returned NSEC TTL exceeds the required lesser value. | No |

## RFC 9110: HTTP Semantics

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.4 Error Handling | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:124-126, 194-196`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607, 653-658, 699-706` | **Violation**: `/health` ignores the method and returns 200 for every method; `/ready` and `/metrics` return 404 for non-GET methods even when the path exists. These handlers discard method semantics; the ready and prometheus READMEs record their 404 behavior, but the health README does not record its method behavior. | No |
| §7.4 Rejecting Misdirected Requests | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-185`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607,653-658,692-705` | **Violation**: The shared listener routes only on `request.uri().path()` and ignores the target URI scheme. An absolute-form `https` target for an operational endpoint is routed over cleartext, contrary to the requirement to reject it unless the connection is secured for that origin. | No |
| §8.8.3.1 Generation | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:186-193`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607,653-658,699-706` | **Violation**: `/ready` and `/metrics` bodies change with check state and metric values, so change detection is reasonable, but the shared response builder adds no ETag. Health's constant `OK` body is exempt. No ADR or plugin README documents this omission. | No |
| §9.1 Overview — case-sensitive method token | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607`, `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-198`, `crates/pharos-kubernetes/examples/support/api.rs:25-27`, `crates/pharos-kubernetes/examples/differential.rs:311-369`, `crates/pharos-kubernetes/examples/memory.rs:103-141` | **Violation**: Health and the Kubernetes mock APIs ignore the method, so lowercase `get` receives the same response as `GET`. This conflates case-sensitive method tokens. | No |
| §9.1 Overview — unrecognized or unimplemented methods | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607,653-659,699-706`, `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:124-126,184-197`, `crates/pharos-kubernetes/examples/support/api.rs:25-27`, `crates/pharos-kubernetes/examples/differential.rs:311-369`, `crates/pharos-kubernetes/examples/memory.rs:103-141` | **Violation**: Health returns its normal response, readiness and metrics return 404, and Kubernetes mock APIs return normal GET responses regardless of method. RFC 9110 recommends 501 for unrecognized or unimplemented methods and 405 for recognized methods not allowed by the resource. | No |
| §9.3.2 HEAD | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:124-126, crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:653-659, 699-706` | **Partial**: Hyper suppresses content for HEAD, but the GET-only ready and metrics routes return 404 instead of the GET route’s status and corresponding headers. | No |
| §9.3.3 POST | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-606` | **Violation**: The health route ignores the method and request representation, then returns 200 `OK` for `POST /health`; it does not process the representation according to POST semantics. | No |
| §9.3.4 PUT | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-606` | **Violation**: The health route ignores the method and request representation, then returns 200 `OK` for `PUT /health`; it does not create or replace target-resource state. | No |
| §9.3.5 DELETE | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-606` | **Violation**: The health route ignores the method and returns 200 `OK` for `DELETE /health` without removing the target resource’s association with its functionality. | No |
| §9.3.8 TRACE | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607, crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-192` | **Violation**: The health route ignores the method and returns constant `OK` for `TRACE /health`; it does not reflect the received request as response content. No source document justifies this. | No |
| §10.1.2 From | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:93-97` | **Violation**: The periodic health self-probe acts as a robotic user agent but sends no `From` contact address. RFC 9110 says robotic user agents SHOULD send a valid address; no exception is documented. | No |
| §10.1.5 User-Agent | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:93-97` | **Violation**: The health self-probe sends no `User-Agent` field, despite RFC 9110's SHOULD to send one on each request unless specifically configured not to. No opt-out is documented. | No |
| §13.1.1 If-Match | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607,653-659,699-706` | **Violation**: Origin servers MUST evaluate If-Match before performing a method, using strong comparison. These handlers ignore request fields; with no ETag, a non-wildcard tag list cannot match, yet the endpoint still serves the representation. | No |
| §13.1.1 If-Match | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607,653-659,699-706` | **Violation**: An origin server MUST NOT perform the method when If-Match is false. A GET with a nonmatching If-Match tag list still receives the normal 200 response instead of a failed precondition. | No |
| §13.1.2 If-None-Match | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607,653-659,699-706` | **Violation**: Recipients MUST evaluate If-None-Match using weak comparison before the method. The handlers ignore it; `If-None-Match: *` matches each existing endpoint representation but still receives 200. | No |
| §13.1.2 If-None-Match | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607,653-659,699-706` | **Violation**: When If-None-Match is false, an origin server MUST NOT perform the method and MUST return 304 for GET/HEAD or 412 otherwise. A matching wildcard gets the normal 200 response. | No |
| §13.2.1 When to Evaluate | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607,653-659,699-706` | **Violation**: An origin server MUST evaluate applicable preconditions after normal checks and before processing content or performing the method when the unconditional response would be 2xx or 412. Existing operational endpoints return 200, but the shared handler ignores all conditional fields. | No |
| §13.2.2 Precedence of Preconditions | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-198` | **Violation**: An origin server MUST evaluate multiple conditional fields in the specified order. The shared handler ignores all conditional fields, including requests carrying multiple preconditions. | No |
| §14.2 Range | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:183-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:699-706` | **Partial**: The listener ignores `Range` and sends complete responses. RFC 9110 makes range requests optional, but says origin servers ought to support byte ranges when possible; `/metrics` can return large representations, and no justification for omitting range support is recorded. | No |
| §14.5 Partial PUT | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:124-126,183-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:597-607,653-659,699-706` | **Violation**: Health returns 200 for every method on `/health`, including `PUT` with `Content-Range`; ready and metrics routes return 404. None returns the SHOULD-recommended 400 for `Content-Range` on `PUT` when partial PUT is unsupported, and no justification is recorded. | No |
| §15.5 Client Error 4xx | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:186-196`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:653-659`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:699-706` | **Partial**: Readiness and metrics path or method misses return empty 404 responses. RFC 9110 says a server SHOULD send an explanatory representation except for HEAD; the health handler supplies a generic not-found explanation. | No |
| §15.5.5 404 Not Found | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:124-126`, `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:196`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:653-659`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:699-706` | **Deviation**: `/ready` and `/metrics` serve GET only, and other methods on those paths receive 404. The prometheus README records "Only GET requests are served at `/metrics`; other HTTP methods return 404", and the ready README records "serves GET only", so the method policy is documented. The 405 that RFC 9110 §9.1 prefers is assessed under §9.1. | Yes |
| §15.6 Server Error 5xx | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:186-194` | **Partial**: Invalid callback status or content type becomes 500 with an empty body. RFC 9110 §15.6 says a server SHOULD send an explanatory representation except for HEAD. | No |
| §15.6 Server Error 5xx | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:186-196`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/ready/mod.rs:76-99` | **Partial**: `/ready` 503 responses name shutdown or unready checks but do not say whether the condition is temporary or permanent. The 500 fallback sends no explanatory representation. | No |
| §17.5 Attacks via Protocol Element Length | `crates/pharos-server/src/http.rs:29-30,159-206` | **Partial**: The listener caps concurrent connections at 1,024 and each connection at five seconds, but configures no request-target or field-size limits; remaining parsing bounds rely on Hyper defaults. | No |

The helper has no production caller; the signer currently emits NSEC instead. | No |

## RFC 9293: Transmission Control Protocol (TCP)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3.7.4 Nagle Algorithm | `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:112`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:70` | **Violation**: SHLD-7 says a TCP implementation SHOULD implement Nagle. Both the DNS-over-TCP server and the proxy upstream always call `set_nodelay(true)` and never enable Nagle. The per-connection disable (MUST-17) is met by the same call. No ADR or plugin README justifies the deviation; the `tcp.rs:111` comment gives a rationale but is not a recorded decision. | Yes |

## RFC 9432: DNS Catalog Zones

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §4 Catalog Zone Structure | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/catalog.rs:68-71,99-101`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:135-157`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:144-198` | **Partial**: The catalog parser requires apex SOA and NS records, and zone construction validates SOA owner and RDATA. It silently keeps only the last SOA when an SOA RRset has multiple records, so an invalid catalog is still processed; the existing citation also names §§4.1 and 5.1 instead of §4. | No |
| §4.2 Properties | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/catalog.rs:92-120` | **Partial**: The parser rejects multiple `coo` PTRs only while iterating member nodes with a matching `zones` PTR. Duplicate `coo` records on an orphan node are left unchecked, so the broken catalog can still be processed. | No |
| §4.3.1 Change of Ownership (coo property) | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/catalog.rs:92-120` | **Violation**: The parser does not enforce the single-record limit for `coo` RRsets without a matching member PTR. RFC 9432 requires such an RRset to make the catalog broken and unprocessable. | No |
| §5.1 General Requirements | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/catalog.rs:133-164`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:145-149` | **Violation**: A broken catalog transfer returns before replacing the cached catalog. A later update from another catalog can still use the stale `coo` property to migrate and reconfigure a member whose source catalog is broken, despite the requirement that its members not be reconfigured. | No |
| §5.1 General Requirements | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/catalog.rs:159-164`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:327` | **Violation**: Expiration only marks the secondary zone stale; cached catalog state remains eligible to authorize migration through its old `coo` property. An expired catalog is therefore still processed as a catalog when another catalog updates. | No |
| §5.1 General Requirements | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/mod.rs:71-80,93-101`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:303-315` | **Partial**: Startup proceeds while the initial transfer retries, but catalog membership and the last valid member-zone snapshots are in memory only. After restart with a broken catalog, Pharos cannot serve member zones from the last valid catalog version. | No |
| §5.2 Member Zone Name Clash | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/catalog.rs:147-155` | **Violation**: Pharos ignores an incoming member when its name already exists, but logs no error for the clash, contrary to the RFC's SHOULD. | No |
| §7 Security Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:87-92`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/setup.rs:38-44` | **Missing**: Catalog AXFR uses `axfr_coredns` without TSIG or TLS/QUIC, and the transfer configuration exposes no authentication option. `pharos_server::transfer::axfr_signed` exists, but the secondary plugin never calls it. | No |
| §7 Security Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:87-92`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/setup.rs:38-44` | **Missing**: Catalog-created member zones use the same unsigned AXFR path, and no authentication option exists for member transfers. | No |
| §7 Security Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/acl/mod.rs:189-314`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:87-92` | **Partial**: Query ACL is optional and not catalog-specific; catalog data remains served as a normal zone. Catalog AXFR also uses plaintext TCP, with no confidential transfer option in this path. | No |
| §7 Security Considerations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/setup.rs:47-71`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/catalog.rs:137-145` | **Partial**: A nonempty `catalog` suffix list restricts admitted members, but an empty list disables the check. The consumer does not require an admissible-zone scope. | No |

## RFC 9460: Service Binding and Parameter Specification via the DNS (SVCB and HTTPS Resource Records)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1 Zone-File Presentation Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone.rs:496,525-526,535-542,640-661` | **Violation**: SvcParam parsing bypasses the malformed-digit-escape check. It accepts `\9` and `\99`, although Appendix A permits only a non-digit escape or a three-digit decimal octet from 000 to 255. | Yes |
| §2.2 RDATA Wire Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:797-799,1104-1106` | **Deviation**: The decoder accepts compressed TargetName values, although §2.2 specifies an uncompressed name. The writer emits it uncompressed; `crates/pharos/compatibility.md` records accepting compressed names under ADR 0002. | Yes |
| §2.4.1 SvcPriority | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:454-457,802-810` | **Violation**: Zone RRsets are returned without checking mode consistency, so a set can mix AliasMode and ServiceMode despite the SHOULD. No justification is recorded. | No |
| §2.4.3 ServiceMode | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:45-61,86-118`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1084-1177`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_strictness.rs:246-249` | **Partial**: Wire and zone-file parsing do not enforce cross-parameter self-consistency. Both accept `no-default-alpn` without `alpn`, contrary to §§2.4.3 and 7.1.1. | No |
| §4.1 Authoritative Servers — Additional target records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:984-1021` | **Partial**: SVCB and HTTPS answers add in-zone A and AAAA target records, but do not add target SVCB records as §4.1 recommends. | No |
| §4.1 Authoritative Servers — DNSSEC records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:445-469`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:1011-1020` | **Partial**: DNSSEC-enabled glue includes RRSIGs for present A and AAAA sets, but does not add SVCB target records or DNSSEC proofs for absent target sets. | No |
| §4.1 Authoritative Servers — TargetName "." | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:993-1008`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:397-399` | **Partial**: §2.5.2 makes the owner name the effective TargetName when TargetName is ".", and §4.1 asks for A and AAAA for in-zone targets. `rdata_target` yields the root name, which `contains` rejects, so no owner-name A or AAAA is added to the Additional section. | No |
| §4.4 EDNS Client Subnet (ECS) | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:985-1020`, `crates/pharos-server/src/server.rs:176-183`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/expression/README.md:17-19` | **Violation**: SVCB and HTTPS answers add in-zone A/AAAA targets without checking whether those records are suitable for source-prefix-zero clients. Views can select response data by `client_ip`, so this path can expose network-specific Additional records; no documented exception covers it. | No |
| §7.1.1 Representation | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:45-61,86-118`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1115-1122`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_strictness.rs:243-249` | **Violation**: Wire parsing accepts `no-default-alpn` without `alpn`, and zone parsing does not enforce that pairing either. Both violate the "self-consistent" requirement for `no-default-alpn`. | No |
| §7.1.1 Representation — empty alpn | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1115-1122`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_strictness.rs:243-244` | **Violation**: The `alpn` presentation value must list one or more alpn-ids, but zone parsing accepts `alpn=` and serializes an empty value. Wire parsing rejects it, so the two paths disagree. | Yes |
| §7.2 "port" | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1128-1135`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:814-821` | **Violation**: `\DDD` escapes are decoded before integer parsing, so escaped digits are accepted although the presentation value must be unescaped ASCII decimal and MUST NOT contain escapes. | No |
| §7.3 "ipv4hint" and "ipv6hint" | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:123-130`; `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1147-1152` | **Violation**: parser rejects IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses. RFC 9460 permits appropriate-family IPv6 addresses in standard textual format, and RFC 5952 §5 defines the mapped form; `compatibility.md` records the rejection but gives no RFC basis. | No |
| §8 ServiceMode RR Compatibility and Mandatory Keys | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:45-60,86-101`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata.rs:1101-1113`, `crates/pharos-proto/tests/zone_strictness.rs:228-241` | **Violation**: Wire parsing accepts mandatory keys absent from SvcParams. Zone-file parsing also serializes empty, invalid, duplicate, self-referential, or absent mandatory keys; its citation incorrectly names §7.1 instead of §8. | No |

## RFC 9520: Negative Caching of DNS Resolution Failures

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3.1 Retries and Timeouts | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/setup.rs:111-123`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:288-303` | **Violation**: `max_connect_attempts` accepts values above three and zero means unlimited. The forwarding loop can therefore send more than three queries to the same upstream over the same transport before its five-second deadline. | No |
| §3.2 Caching | `crates/pharos-proto/src/response.rs:88-95`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:625-634` | **Partial**: The failure cache stores SERVFAIL and internal errors, but REFUSED is classified as `OtherError` and excluded. RFC 9520 §2.2 identifies REFUSED as a resolution-failure condition. | No |
| §3.2 Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:201-209`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:532-533` | **Partial**: The `servfail` option accepts zero and subsecond durations; processing converts the duration to whole seconds and skips insertion at zero. Operators can therefore disable failure caching or set it below the one-second minimum. The citation at `setup.rs:206` names §3.2 but covers only the five-minute cap. | Yes |
| §3.2 Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:139-150`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:582-585`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/store.rs:163-171` | **Partial**: `servfail` limits the normal failure TTL to five minutes, but `serve_stale` also serves expired entries from the denial store, including SERVFAIL entries, for its separately configured stale interval. This can make a cached failure available beyond five minutes. | No |
| §3.2 Caching | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/setup.rs:24,66`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/mod.rs:532-533,619-620` | **Violation**: Every failure uses the same configured TTL; repeated failures do not increase the cache duration with linear or exponential backoff. | No |
| §3.2 Caching | — | **Missing**: The SHOULD that developers document failure-cache choices for operators applies, but `crates/pharos-plugins/src/cache/README.md` describes negative-response TTLs only and no failure-cache conditions, keys or TTLs. | No |

## RFC 9606: DNS Resolver Information

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §3 Retrieving Resolver Information — RRset cardinality | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:258-267,445-458` | **Violation**: RESINFO is recognized as a record type, but zone lookup and response writing emit every matching record. No rule limits a RESINFO RRset to exactly one record. | No |
| §4 Format of the Resolver Information — key names | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:164-172` | **Partial**: RESINFO fields are accepted as generic strings without checking that each key is registered or begins with `temp-`; invalid keys can be served from a zone. | No |
| §4 Format of the Resolver Information — key/value syntax | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:164-172` | **Partial**: RESINFO presentation data is encoded as TXT-style character-strings. The parser does not validate that each string follows the DNS-SD key/value syntax. | No |
| §5 Resolver Information Keys/Values — exterr value syntax | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/legacy.rs:164-172` | **Partial**: `exterr` values are stored as opaque character-strings. The parser does not check that EDE ranges use `-` or that multiple values are comma-separated. | No |

## RFC 9824: Compact Denial of Existence in DNSSEC

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2 Distinguishing Nonexistent Names | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:171-214` | **Partial**: `NXNAME` (type 128) is defined and `type_bitmap` can encode it, but generated black-lie NSEC bitmaps omit it and therefore do not signal nonexistent names as specified. | No |
| §3.1 Responses for Nonexistent Names | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:99-108,151-156,262-289` | **Partial**: For recognized denials, the signer changes the RCODE to NOERROR and adds a synthetic NSEC to Authority. Responses without exactly one SOA are returned unchanged as NXDOMAIN with no NSEC, so they get no compact denial. The Answer section is already empty for denials, because `typify_response` never reports a denial when answers are present. | No |
| §3.1 Responses for Nonexistent Names | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:171-214` | **Violation**: The NSEC bitmap contains additional RR types and omits NXNAME; §3.1 requires only RRSIG, NSEC, and NXNAME. No RFC 9824-specific justification for this difference is recorded. | No |
| §3.1 Responses for Nonexistent Names | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/setup.rs:27-28,97-105`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:203-204,262-289` | **Partial**: The plugin signs the synthetic NSEC when keys are configured, but setup permits an empty key list and signing is gated on keys being present; that path emits no corresponding RRSIG. | No |
| §3.2 Responses for Nonexistent Types | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:171-194` | **Violation**: NODATA NSEC bitmaps come from a fixed RR-type list with the queried type removed, not the RR types actually available at the owner name as required. | No |
| §3.5 Responses to Explicit Queries for NXNAME | — | **Missing**: A DNS server MUST return FORMERR for an explicit QTYPE 128 query, but no code checks for it; the query reaches the plugin chain and gets a normal zone, forward, or signer answer. | No |

## RFC 9904: DNSSEC Cryptographic Algorithm Recommendation Update Process

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §1.2 Updating Algorithm Requirement Levels | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/sign.rs:80-101`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:129-131,200-203`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:87-117` | **Partial**: Signing rejects algorithms 5 and 7, but accepts algorithm 10 (RSASHA512), which RFC 9904 §3 marks NOT RECOMMENDED for signing; §1.2 advises signers not to use algorithms at that level to create DNSKEYs. | No |
| §3 DNS Security Algorithm Numbers Registry Column Values | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/sign.rs:45-52`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:129-148`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:200-204` | **Violation**: Pharos accepts and signs with algorithm 10 (RSASHA512), although §3 marks its signing use and implementation NOT RECOMMENDED. No local exception is documented. | No |
| §4 Digest Algorithms Registry Column Values | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/digest.rs:38-65`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:97` | **Partial**: The signer emits only SHA-256 CDS (signer.rs:97), but public `cds_rdata` still implements SHA-1 (digest.rs:41,58), which §4 marks MUST NOT for DNSSEC delegation implementation. `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/README.md:7` records the signer omission but cites RFC 9905 §2, not this section, and no exception covers the crate API. | No |

## RFC 9905: Deprecating the Use of SHA-1 in DNSSEC Signature Algorithms

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §5 IANA Considerations — Digest Algorithms | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/digest.rs:6-9`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/digest.rs:38-49`, `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/digest.rs:54-65` | **Violation**: `DigestType::Sha1` implements SHA-1 DS/CDS digest generation and maps it to digest type 1, despite the registry's `Implement for DNSSEC Delegation: MUST NOT`. The signing plugin selects SHA-256; this finding concerns the public SHA-1 digest API, not NSEC3 chain hashing. | No |

## KEP-1645: Multi-Cluster Services API

| KEP-1645 §specification.md > 2.1 - Definitions | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/setup.rs:185-190,237-243` | **Violation**: `multicluster` accepts configured zones other than `clusterset.local`; the specification requires that zone. No recorded justification permits custom zones. | No |
| KEP-1645 §specification.md > 2.2 - Record for Schema Version | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1008-1017` | **Deviation**: The `dns-version` TXT record returns `1.1.0`, while the MCS DNS schema declares `1.0.0`. The plugin README documents this as CoreDNS parity. | Yes |
| KEP-1645 §specification.md > 2.3.1 - `A`/`AAAA` Record | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:533-542,1128-1158` | **Deviation**: The handler emits family-matched ClusterSetIP addresses, including both families when present, but emits none when a ServiceImport has no ports because address enumeration is nested under the port loop; the plugin README records this CoreDNS-compatible NXDOMAIN. | Yes |
| KEP-1645 §specification.md > 2.3.2 - `SRV` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:124-135`; `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:340-346,1128-1203` | **Deviation**: Named ports produce SRV records and unnamed ports do not. An omitted ServiceImport protocol becomes empty instead of TCP, so it does not match `_tcp`; the plugin README records this CoreDNS-compatible difference. | Yes |
| KEP-1645 §specification.md > 2.4.1 - `A`/`AAAA` Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/name.rs:47-62`; `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:507-525`; `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:243-260` | **Partial**: KEP-1645 permits cluster IDs made from two DNS labels separated by a dot (`target/conformance/specs/kep-1645.md:275-282`). The multicluster query parser accepts one endpoint label and one cluster label, then rejects additional labels, so hostname-qualified A/AAAA records for valid two-label cluster IDs cannot be queried. | Yes |
| KEP-1645 §specification.md > 2.4.2 - `SRV` Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1165-1180,301-315`; `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:255-260` | **Partial**: KEP-1645 permits cluster IDs made from two DNS labels separated by a dot, but `child` encodes the entire `source-cluster` value as one label. SRV targets for those IDs therefore differ from `<hostname>.<clusterid>...` and cannot be queried at the specified name. | Yes |

## Kubernetes DNS spec

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2 Resource Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:340-346, 547-554, 813-838, 1083-1097`; `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221` | **Partial**: Pharos provides many required record types, but omits required records in cases including ignored empty ClusterIP services, tolerated unready endpoints, and failed ExternalName lookups. It also emits SRV answers for unnamed ports, which §2.3.2 excludes (row §2.3.2). | No |
| §2.1 Definitions | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221` | **Partial**: EndpointSlice readiness filters endpoints marked unready, but Pharos drops Service annotations and cannot include them when `service.alpha.kubernetes.io/tolerate-unready-endpoints=true`. The nearby citation names §2.4.1, not this definition. | No |
| §2.3 Record for a Service with ClusterIP | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:547-554` | **Violation**: With `ignore empty_service`, a non-headless Service with no endpoint addresses is treated as absent, so required ClusterIP records are omitted. ADR 0002 gives only a general compatibility policy, and the plugin README does not record this exact behavior. | No |
| §2.3.1 `A`/`AAAA` Record — IPv4 | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:547-554, 605-613, 1136-1158` | **Violation**: With `ignore empty_service`, the service lookup stops before the ClusterIP address writer, so the required IPv4 `A` record is missing when no endpoint addresses exist. ADR 0002 gives only a general compatibility policy; the plugin README does not record this exact behavior. | Yes |
| §2.3.1 `A`/`AAAA` Record — IPv6 | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:547-554, 605-613, 1136-1158` | **Violation**: With `ignore empty_service`, the service lookup stops before the ClusterIP address writer, so the required IPv6 `AAAA` record is missing when no endpoint addresses exist. ADR 0002 gives only a general compatibility policy; the plugin README does not record this exact behavior. | Yes |
| §2.3.2 `SRV` Records — unnamed ports | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:340-346, 1128-1203`; `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/transfer.rs:241-255, 328-335` | **Violation**: A bare service-name `SRV` query accepts unnamed ports, and AXFR emits base SRV records before checking port names. §3 permits extensions, but does not override §2.3.2's explicit exclusion of SRV records for unnamed ports. | Yes |
| §2.4 Records for a Headless Service | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:567-615` | **Partial**: Headless records use ready endpoint addresses, but Pharos drops Service annotations and cannot honor `service.alpha.kubernetes.io/tolerate-unready-endpoints=true`. | No |
| §2.4.1 `A`/`AAAA` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:567-615`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1128-1158` | **Partial**: IPv4 endpoint A records are emitted, but endpoints marked unready remain excluded even when the Service annotation requires tolerating them. | Yes |
| §2.4.1 `A`/`AAAA` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:804-810`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1128-1158` | **Partial**: An empty ready-endpoint set yields NXDOMAIN for an IPv4 A query, but annotated unready endpoints should count as ready and prevent that answer. | Yes |
| §2.4.1 `A`/`AAAA` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:378-407`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:591-603` | **Partial**: Hostname-specific IPv4 A records use endpoint labels, but annotated unready endpoints are omitted. | Yes |
| §2.4.1 `A`/`AAAA` Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:567-603`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1128-1158` | **Partial**: Multiple IPv4 addresses for one endpoint hostname are emitted, except addresses on annotated unready endpoints. | Yes |
| §2.4.1 `A`/`AAAA` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:567-615`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1128-1158` | **Partial**: IPv6 endpoint AAAA records are emitted, but endpoints marked unready remain excluded even when the Service annotation requires tolerating them. | Yes |
| §2.4.1 `A`/`AAAA` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:804-810`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1128-1158` | **Partial**: An empty ready-endpoint set yields NXDOMAIN for an IPv6 AAAA query, but annotated unready endpoints should count as ready and prevent that answer. | Yes |
| §2.4.1 `A`/`AAAA` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:378-407`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:591-603` | **Partial**: Hostname-specific IPv6 AAAA records use endpoint labels, but annotated unready endpoints are omitted. | Yes |
| §2.4.1 `A`/`AAAA` Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:567-603`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1128-1158` | **Partial**: Multiple IPv6 addresses for one endpoint hostname are emitted, except addresses on annotated unready endpoints. | Yes |
| §2.4.2 `SRV` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:567-603`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1159-1204` | **Partial**: Named-port SRV records target endpoint labels, but annotated unready endpoints are omitted from the required endpoint/port combinations. | Yes |
| §2.4.2 `SRV` Records | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/lookup.rs:125-147`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/k8s_external/mod.rs:312-315` | **Partial**: When the query has no port label, `matches` accepts unnamed EndpointSlice ports and the response path emits their SRV records. The spec says unnamed ports have no SRV record. | No |
| §2.4.3 `PTR` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:418-465` | **Partial**: IPv4 endpoint PTRs use endpoint labels, but annotated unready endpoints are omitted. A matching Service ClusterIP also returns early before endpoint PTRs are visited. | Yes |
| §2.4.3 `PTR` Records | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:418-465` | **Partial**: IPv6 endpoint PTRs use endpoint labels, but annotated unready endpoints are omitted. A matching Service ClusterIP also returns early before endpoint PTRs are visited. | Yes |
| §2.5 Records for External Name Services | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:813-838`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:1083-1097` | **Partial**: ExternalName CNAMEs are returned for ordinary query types, but A/AAAA queries omit the required CNAME when Self Lookup fails. | Yes |
| §3 Schema Extensions | `crates/pharos-kubernetes/src/object.rs:193-221`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/kubernetes/mod.rs:567-615` | **Partial**: The subset rule is not met: tolerated unready endpoints can leave required headless records absent, and `ignore empty_service` drops required ClusterIP records (rows §2.3.1, §2.4.1). | No |

| — | `crates/pharos-corefile/src/parser.rs:358-364` | **Violation**: Import patterns with multiple `*` or `?` wildcards, or bracket expressions, are rejected before glob expansion. This conflicts with ADR 0003's promise to accept CoreDNS Corefiles, and no exception is recorded. | No |
| — | `crates/pharos-corefile/src/parser.rs:133-161` | **Deviation**: Environment expansion rejects cycles and stops at 10,000 replacements or 16 MiB, unlike CoreDNS's unbounded self-reference loop; `docs/compatibility.md` General records this difference, as ADR 0002 requires. | Yes |
| — | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:90-176` | **Missing**: the inventory has no normative section for the BIND `.private` v1.2/v1.3 file grammar parsed here; cited algorithm RFCs provide examples, not a grammar specification. | No |
| — | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/auto/setup.rs:65-74` | **Deviation**: `directory` patterns use Rust regex syntax, including Unicode-aware `\d` and `\w`, rather than CoreDNS's Go RE2 syntax. The plugin README records this difference; no in-scope protocol document governs this configuration syntax. | Yes |
| — | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/autopath/mod.rs:26-57,106-125,164-267`; `crates/pharos-plugins/src/autopath/setup.rs:1-14` | **Deviation**: Configured search-path parsing and server-side suffix probing have no governing section in the in-scope standards. The README describes the CoreDNS-compatible extension and several intentional differences. | Yes |
| — | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/gotemplate/quote.rs:36-55` | **Unmapped**: Valid Go rune constants such as `'\xFF'` decode to a single byte, then fail UTF-8 conversion before the caller can consume the rune. The README documents this restriction for string literals, not rune constants. | No |

| — | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/siit/mod.rs:136-140, 153-181, 192-234` | **Unmapped**: this plugin performs reverse synthesis: an IN A query with no A answer triggers an AAAA self-lookup, then maps matching AAAA answers to A. RFC 6147 defines the opposite DNS64 direction (AAAA from A); the `RFC 6147 §5.1` comment at line 136 therefore cites a section that does not govern this gate. The README identifies the behavior as a CoreDNS `siit` port. | No |

| — | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/view/mod.rs:9-33`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/view/README.md:7-9` | **Unmapped**: Per-thread evaluation storage is capped at 65,535 bytes, so expressions needing more storage do not match. | — | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/view/mod.rs:27-32` | **Unmapped**: A view matches only when every expression returns boolean `true`; evaluation errors and scratch-borrow conflicts also become non-matches.
